# ARENA ENGINE — Chronicle archive (FROZEN)

**Not part of the reading order.** GAMES.md's build chronicle and its v0.8.3 measured facts were moved here verbatim on 2026-10-19 at the owner's word ("maybe remove the chronicle, its becoming too long") so the living file stays small. Nothing in here is current game state — for that, read `GAMES.md`. Historical value: owner quotes, taste records, physics disclosures, law calibration/acceptance history, the merge ledger. Internal pointers like "above" or "Measured facts" refer to the OLD GAMES.md layout of 2026-10-18. On 2026-10-22 two superseded Measured-facts entries were retired here verbatim at the owner-ordered doc trim ("remove parts/trim things we no longer need") — see **Retired GAMES.md entries** at the end; the archive stays append-only.

The roster described in the archived material (4 weapons: hammer, dagger, axe, sling) is gone: the sling was replaced by the bow (v0.9) and the roster grew to 13 (v1.0–v1.1) during an undocumented era the owner drove directly in the preview — "the file spinclash.html was updated by my, but i didnt write anything in the docs" (owner, 2026-10-19). After the archived material follow: the reconstruction of that era, the record of the v1.2 alignment session, and the superseded normative sections (the sling/hold-styles doctrine and the 2026-10-18 weapon-art lock) preserved verbatim.

---

## Measured facts (40 seeds + forced 16-cell matrix, headless, preview build v0.8.3 — 2026-10-18)

v0.8.3 = v0.8.2 (renderer-only) + the axe replacement (art + len merge; the sim path is untouched). Full re-baseline with a surgical proof built in: **every forced cell WITHOUT the axe is byte-identical to v0.8.1/v0.8.2** — only the axe cells moved (the bigger axe leans speedy). Law numbers live per rule in **Core contact law** and **Projectile weapon** above; measured inside every `measure.mjs` run: 170 battles / 398 rounds / 6557 hits / 4621 parries / 12720 stones / 25.1M substep poses / zero determinism drift / every check green except the stall alarm.

**v0.8.3:** 40/40 terminate with a winner · determinism spot-check passed · winner spread **heavy 24 / speedy 16** · 19 two-round battles, 21 three-round · battle median **107.1s**, mean 121.5s, max 294.7s (≥60s in 37/40) · round median 37.9s, mean 45.9s, max 185.3s · hits 16.6/round, parries 11.2/round · event rate ~4.4/s · combat stall median 3.75s, p90 4.61s, **worst 5.29s — FAIL vs the 5s alarm (open question 1; improved from 6.48s, still red)** · max spin median 7.1, p90 21.5, max 23.5 rad/s; **buzzsaw (≥25) in 0/40** · max damage 19.

**Stones (v0.8.3):** 12720 thrown · 1066 landed (8.4%) · 658 swatted (5.2%) · 10731 rim deaths (84.4%) · 106 crossed · 93 cleared at round rolls · 0 unaccounted. Every landing and swat recomputed to exact contact; zero rendered-frame stone overlaps over 686k stone-bearing frames; all launches recomputed to the spine middle (law 6s); 0 melee hits by ranged spines (law 7s, 2554 zero-damage shoves flag-audited).

**Combo matrix (v0.8.3, FORCED — 8 shared seeds per ordered cell; Speedy-weapon + Heavy-weapon → rounds, speedy/heavy split, median round sec):** axe+axe 20r 8/12 31.4 · axe+dagger 20r 13/7 32.0 · axe+hammer 20r 5/15 27.8 · axe+sling 16r 16/0 42.1 · dagger+axe 19r 3/16 30.9 · dagger+dagger 18r 2/16 41.8 · dagger+hammer 18r 2/16 29.0 · dagger+sling 17r 16/1 63.0 · hammer+axe 21r 11/10 29.4 · hammer+dagger 19r 16/3 29.0 · hammer+hammer 20r 7/13 27.6 · hammer+sling 16r 16/0 48.0 · sling+axe 16r 0/16 33.5 · sling+dagger 17r 1/16 44.0 · sling+hammer 16r 0/16 33.8 · sling+sling 17r 4/13 **116.2** (longest cell — stone-only damage). Read: every mixed sling cell still goes to the non-sling side; the sling mirror owns the clock; the axe's heavy lean softened toward speedy at hammer size (axe+dagger 9/10 → 13/7, hammer+axe 7/12 → 11/10) while the hammer cells keep theirs; the buzzsaw stays absent. Frozen until the balance pass (open question 1).

## Build chronicle

Every build from v0.8 step 1 on is listed below — no entry dropped; the earlier builds' decisions (v0.5 → v0.7.3) live in the normative sections they produced (laws, Sling, Hold styles, Locked frame). Superseded per-step baseline numbers are deliberately not kept: the live baseline is in **Measured facts**, the law acceptance/calibration history under **Core contact law**. What the entries preserve is every owner decision (with quotes), the taste record, the physics disclosures and the merge ledger.

**v0.8 step-1 series (2026-10-17 → 2026-10-18): path silhouettes + the paste-ingest path + the house weapon style.** Owner mandate: "i just want to be able to paste [an SVG] and we can seamlessly add it in the game without breaking anything." Step 1 of the complex-silhouette fork (approved after the AI-concept translations hit the 1-D spine ceiling). The doctrine these steps produced — palette, construction grammar, proportions, legibility, supplier workflow — lives normatively in **Weapon art conventions**; this entry keeps the decisions, the taste record, the disclosures and the ledger.

**step1a — the representation + the gate.** The owner-pasted axe (variant A) landed as a fourth weapon: polygons verbatim from the SVG (its ink stroke, 3u, counts as body), collision = a greedy maximal-inscribed circle covering (27 circles, min r 2.5), uncovered-sliver epsilon disclosed (1.07u). Physics disclosures (still true): the collision solid is the notch-FILLED union (the cavity mouth, 3.4u, is narrower than any blade circle, so every incursion visually contacts both lips — the drawing keeps the notch); multi-contact sequential impulses (wide heads crossing in an X jam single-normal pushes); substeps sized to the documented rule, travel ≤ half the narrowest window (the old +parryPad slack let one substep bury contact clusters 5px deep); pin escape — a scissor burial with bodies touching is translation-infeasible, so at iteration 3 with gap < −1 the resolver nudges each wAngle 0.15 rad along its own spin (sign preserved; law 5 audits spin sign, never wAngle), disclosed as momentum carrying the blades through the pin. **`ingest.mjs` born as the paste path** (now the merge gate; full pipeline in the Testing recipe): proven at birth — an icon-style glyph rejects on aspect, the owner's axe validates → gauntlets green → merges; refusals are the default and are loud. Reds recorded, owner-deferred (open question 1, not step-1 breakage): the stall alarm (worst 6.07s, standing); seed-13 sling+sling stone-starvation stalemate, termination 39/40 — bodies 498u apart, zero blade contact, so neither solver nor representation causal (the tail fell away at step1c; the disease class stayed deferred, not cured); and a proportion note — the pasted axe's head half-width ~10u vs the hand-built references' 24–27u reads as a hatchet at arena scale; if it graduates, the balance pass rescales the head or the owner accepts the hatchet read.

**step1b — the axe v2 paste.** The owner's second paste replaced variant A: single-bit crescent head with two open rings, gold bands, verbatim at len 96 — 7 fill polygons, holes carried as even-odd subpaths so the rings stay real holes in drawing AND collision, 28 covering circles, ε 0.74u, six narrow concave columns filled in the collision solid per the disclosed rule. The swap exposed two ingest bugs, now permanent rules: source outlines are raster-only (they inflate the covering) and never draw polys (a stroke centreline drawn as a fill swallows thin color bands); multi-subpath shapes fill even-odd so holes survive. Gauntlet 20 battles green before the merge; the deferred reds unchanged.

**step1c — four owner decisions + a perf pass (playtest seed 69737).** (1) **Weapons always draw above every body** — draw() is two passes; the own-grip tuck now reads on top of the own disc. (2) **Spin trails and parry ghosts retired from the locked frame** — the growth tell lives only in the legend's SPD number; if the owner misses a visual tell, that is a new design decision, not a restoration. (3) **Legend weapon meta is dynamic** (built from WEAPONS at boot — ingested weapons no longer show "rolling…" mid-fight). (4) **Perf:** bounce events throttled to one per tick (per-substep churn fed GC pauses that read as slow-motion); the frame loop became a real-time catch-up accumulator (whole ticks owed to the wall clock, capped 10/frame) — an overrun frame can never slow-motion the preview again; resolver iterations 16→10 + a parryGap axis prefilter (mean 0.05–0.16 ms/tick, p99 ≤0.5 ms; rare 40–50 ms GC/pin tails disclosed, masked by the catch-up). Re-proof: termination back to 40/40, laws green, stall alarm the only red.

**step1d — the frame goes quiet, weapons scale up, the solver finishes converging.** (1) **All white contact-pose ghosts retired** (hit, stone, swat — on top of step1c's parry ghosts): the struck body's white flash is now the frame's only hit punctuation, parry reads purely physically (recoil + shove), and proof-of-contact lives entirely in the harness — the frame is quiet by owner taste. (2) **`CFG.weaponScale = 1.3`** — owner: weapons read small against the bodyR-64 discs. One boot-time uniform scale on lengths, part radii, poly coordinates, covering circles and the sling's stone radius (drawn pouch disc = launched hitbox stays true); effective lengths hammer 143 / dagger 96.2 / sling 80.6 / axe 124.8. Supersedes the round-2 frozen-lengths rule by owner decision; the knob is one number, ~6 min re-baseline per turn. (3) Solver finished: adaptive resolver iterations (project while progress > 0.02px); pin escape relocated into stall detection and directed by the contact torque's own sign (the spin-direction guess could rotate blades deeper — 9.7px transients at 12 seeds); and a real audit bug fixed — parry events stored `a.wAngle` at emit time (post-nudge), so the law-2 recompute read a pose that never existed (791/4792 phantom slack); entry angles are now captured pre-resolver like entry positions. `measure.mjs` law 3 became scoped by representation class (parts and path carry different disclosed tolerances; one shared line double-counted). The scale moved the spread toward speedy (11/29 → 18/22) and surfaced a >300s sling-mirror battle — the **>300s length check joined the stall alarm as a standing red** (green again by step1h; recorded, not judged).

**step1e — the house style locks.** Palette and construction grammar locked for every weapon on the channel, the **sole-supplier / sole-selector workflow** adopted (owner: browsing externally is out, consistency of style is the goal) and weaponScale kept 1.3 provisionally — the normative text is **Weapon art conventions**; recorded here as the decision point (it settled the standing cool-vs-warm question in favour of the reference family the owner kept choosing; the PEBBLE frame stays warm around it). Cleanup: dead `drawFighter`, stale ghost comments, ingest header reframed to the merge-gate role. Art state at the time: axe grandfathered (pre-palette paste); hammer/dagger/sling legacy capsules awaiting authored batches — the frame mixed two art families, the exact debt this series paid off.

**step1f–1k — the hammer and dagger taste record (five revision rounds to v5).** The grammar that ended up locked lives in **Weapon art conventions**; this is the record of the owner's eye getting there. **v1 hammer (step1f):** candidate A picked from the first authored batch, two revisions — no bright face band (near-white stays *optional* per weapon, never defaulted on; a white block on a head reads as a flaw to the owner's eye), head rectangular and a bit wider than the maul flare. **step1g added:** the **tuck rule** (owner decision: the butt tip never draws over the own disc — the weapon pass clips the own body circle out, even-odd; handle-less future weapons (shuriken class) carry `tuck: false` or the clip erases them) with its grammar addendum (the first ~60 grip units hide in play — butts plain, handle detail from the guard outwards); the **ingest orientation fix** ("thinner end = grip" mirrored every tapered-tip design → *an end tapering to ~zero width is the tip; only when neither end tapers does the thinner blunt end read as the grip*); and the **scout workflow** (external blocks are silhouette ideas only, redrawn into house grammar, picked from one unlabeled contact sheet — the first scout — a v4-prompt cage-pommel dagger with two open holes, evaluated VALID at ε 1.10u / 12 circles — joined the batch as candidate E after my redraw gave the blade's flat top edge a belly). **v2 dagger (step1h):** candidate A, three revisions — blade shortened to ~59% of total and slimmed slightly (half-width 68→56), handle rebuilt around the tuck rule (plain hidden butt, amber pommel collar just outside the rim, one wrap band on the visible haft, guard outwards); doctrine added: dagger and future sword must read apart at a glance. **v3 "wielded" (step1i):** owner — shafts too long relative to the head (didn't read as wielded), everything too thin, and low-shaft detail near the body reads as clutter (the dagger's amber collar) → grammar: shaft plain wood body-to-guard with zero detail, head/blade starts by 45–50% of total, chunk targets haft ~7–9% of length full width and head/blade half-width ~18–25% (v2 was roughly half that — why it read thin). **v4 (step1j):** owner — shafts still thin, and the hammer head **"going vertically is wrong, does not look like a hammer"** (the axial block read as a slab; the owner's mental hammer is a lateral rectangle, wide on both sides of the haft, a double-faced sledge block) → lateral heads, shafts ~10–11u (hammer) / ~9u (dagger). **v5 shipped (step1k):** three owner rules, now house grammar — universal shaft width (the hammer v4 shaft is the standard for every hafted weapon; the dagger matches), lateral head ≥ ~1.4:1 wider across than along (v4's near-square head read "vertical" at half the rotations; v5 = 340 lateral vs 240 axial), dagger stub grip (~15% visible shaft beyond the disc; a long handle on a small weapon read wrong) — differentiation by total length + stoutness; the old 55–60% blade-share cap retired. **Bug classes closed:** the hammer replacement left a duplicate id in WEAPON_IDS (entry stripped, id not), doubling the hammer's dice weight — **the step1f/1g baselines were rolled under that skew and are SUPERSEDED** — and after an orphan-const/duplicate-id incident at v3, ingest refuses existing ids and existing merge consts (replacements strip entry + id + const explicitly).

**Merge ledger (every merge through the gate, gauntlet PASS; losers stripped at lock; sources of record `INGR_HAMMER` / `INGR_DAGGER` / `INGR_AXE` in the preview — hammer/dagger source SVGs purged at the 2026-10-18 owner cleanup; the axe2-D source + contact sheet purged at the same cleanup on the owner's word):** axe v1 (27 circles, ε 1.07u) → axe v2 (28, ε 0.74u, G20) → hammer v1 (44, ε 1.28u, G20; legacy capsule hammer deleted; its rectangular head's corner pins measured 25.7px → pin escape became depth-scaled: 0.15 rad + 0.02 per px of burial, cap 0.6) → dagger v2 (14, ε 1.24u, G16; legacy capsule dagger deleted) → v3 pair (G9+16) → v4 pair (G12+16) → v5 pair (G12+16) → **axe2-D** (v0.8.3, len 110 per the owner's hammer-size instruction: 32 circles, ε 2.00u — visible component 1.40u, 22 notch columns, G16; replacement flow — `AXE_POLY` + entry + id stripped first; the gate's covering-grid self-sizing fix is disclosed in the v0.8.3 entry).

**Still open from step1f (engine-era, recorded — not fixed): weapon reach vs arena bounds.** Legacy and current rosters both swing past the inscribed circle AND past the 1000-square at rim contact (hammer: body clamp 436 + reach ~226 = 662 from centre vs 500 half-width; the v0.8.3 axe shares the hammer's len-110 class, so it shares the bound); CONTRACT compliance check 6 will refuse this at engine time, so the boundary decision (confine bodies per reach, enlarge the arena, or cap reach) joins the Phase 3 layout work.

**v0.8.1 review pass (owner-requested code review, 2026-10-18): cleanup plus three soundness fixes; baseline moved.** Removed as dead: the whole `parryCd` chain (it gated nothing since the parry-ghost retirement made its last envisioned consumer vanish — audit stride 15 → 13, measure's index constants updated); stacked substep-sizing comments consolidated, `drawWeapon`'s signature simplified, `bounceT` initialised. Fixed in the sim path (fingerprints moved on 39/40 seeds — a re-baseline, not a bit-identical pass): (1) `REACH` is the DRAWN outer radius from the body centre for both representations (parts tips budgeted tip speed at circle-centre distance, under-budgeting by ω·r_tip — the v0.5.1 tunneling class in miniature); (2) parryGap's body-distance prune unified over ALL pairs at that outer reach (the poly-only variant under-bounded poly-vs-parts contacts); (3) the segment prune applies only to spine-hugging parts pairs — path-weapon circles sit laterally off their spine (axe bits, bow limbs), where MAXR-around-the-segment does not bound them and the prune could miss real contacts (latent, never fired in 40 seeds, now impossible). Resolver otherwise untouched. Law-3 parts teeth recalibrated 1.0 → 1.25px with traced attribution (details in law 3's acceptance history). **The current baseline in Measured facts is this run.** Representation state: the sling is the ONLY parts-representation weapon — step 2 shrinks to "sling batch lands as paths, then delete the parts representation entirely". Harness note: scratch probes reading auditSubs must use stride 13 — a stale-index probe briefly reported a vacuous zero here, caught by cross-checking against measure.

**v0.8.2 (2026-10-18): render interpolation — even motion on any display.** Owner reported judder in the preview (mixed-look content, non-60Hz-class display, at normal speed). Reproduced headlessly before patching, per house rule: `.qa/pacing.mjs` proved it was pacing, not cost — the catch-up loop hands whole ticks to frames, so a display whose refresh is not an integer multiple of the 120 Hz tick redraws the same pose on leftover frames: **16.6% dead frames at 144 Hz** (the 0,1,1,1,1,1 pattern), 27.3% at 165 Hz, **50% at 240 Hz**; 60/120 Hz smooth. `.qa/tickcost.mjs` exonerated the sim (mean tick 0.117 ms, p50 0.08, p99 0.39 ms; the residual 30–50 ms one-off tails are GC/OS class — contained by the existing 50 ms clamp + cap 10, never eliminable in a browser). The fix is the render-interpolation pattern from the owner's own **`sumo_war (2).html`** (credited reference implementation; analyzed, not modified) — the shipped mechanism is recorded in **Renderer facts**, the rendering-semantics trade in the **pose amendment** under the Core contact law. Two loop decisions: **debt shed when the catch-up cap engages** (`tickAcc %= 1` — after a long stall the sim resumes smoothly instead of burst-ticking) adopted from the reference; redraw-only-on-change NOT adopted (our draw is ~20 calls over a prerendered arena — invisible saving, house rule on over-engineering). Sim/laws/RNG untouched: the new fields are pure copies no law or audit reads. Verification: `.qa/interp.mjs` (retained; replicates the shipped loop math at 60/144/240 Hz × 4 seeds) — dead frames **16.7% → 0.01% at 144 Hz, 50% → 0.02% at 240 Hz**; displacement evenness (CV) converges to the 60 Hz value at every rate (0.366 vs 0.364 — the remaining variance is real gameplay motion); sanity brackets all green (viewA in range, no angle overshoot or long-way-round blends, roll/start snaps exact, stone bases finite and in-arena, frozen phases exact, same-seed trails identical). Chromecheck green. Full 40-seed `measure.mjs` re-run: **every baseline number byte-identical to v0.8.1** — the baseline carries forward.

**v0.8.3 (2026-10-18): the axe replacement — the first fully authored axe (axe2-D), sized to the hammer.** The owner pasted a stock flat-vector double-bit battle-axe image as the redesign target, preview-only first ("dont code or implement it, i just want to see first"): a batch of four candidates (`axe2-a..d`) plus one contact sheet — row 1 same-length big, row 2 as-played true scale (124.8px, tuck clip on) — all four dry-run VALID at len 96 (ε 1.01–1.28u, 23–37 circles, grip-left, single component). Owner pick and size: **"i like D, replace it with the current axe and make it the same height as hammer on the bots"** → ingested at **len 110** — the hammer's len, 143px as played at weaponScale 1.3 (the owner-paste axe v2 was len 96 → 124.8px). D = forward double-bit head: opposed crescent bits welded onto a steel slab, amber socket band, bright edge slivers, plain haft, amber pommel at the butt just outside the rim. Identity stats untouched (ω 3.5, dmg 5, +0.7 damage per damaging hit). The step1a "hatchet read" proportion note retires with the paste: the authored head's half-spread is 27.6u — the 24–27u reference class the note wanted.

**Gate fix, disclosed.** Ingest's covering grid had fixed ±28u lateral rows; at len 110 D's ink-inflated crescent horns reach ±29.1u — rim coverage would pack blind and ε under-measure (1.53u on the clipped grid). The grid now self-sizes from the art (epsilons recorded before the fix stand for their merges). Honest numbers: **32 circles, min r 2.0, ε 2.00u, 22 notch-filled columns** — the wedge channels between crescent backs and socket/slab, the disclosed class (28 columns at len 96: mouth widths scale with len, and the widest stay genuinely open in drawing AND collision at 110). Independent finer-grid re-measure (`.qa/epslocate.mjs`, retained): the worst VISIBLE sliver is **1.40u at a horn-tip ink cap** (d≈75, l≈−12.6); the circles reach |l|+r 29.5 against the 29.13 ink edge, and tip coverage is flush (max d+r 111.0 vs 111.02) — the 2.00−1.40 excess is invisible filled-notch wedge (collision-solid only; the drawing keeps the cavity). The ε class moved above the historic 0.74–1.28u band; levers if it ever matters: blunter horn caps, or an RMIN 1.5 repack (more circles, tighter substep budget).

**Merge.** Replacement surgery per the step1f/1k rule (ingest refuses duplicates): `AXE_POLY` + its stale prototype comment + the entry + the id stripped first; chromecheck PASS on the three-weapon interim; then the gate — VALID → gauntlet 16 battles green (4 seeds × vs sling/hammer/dagger/axe) → merged `INGR_AXE`; chromecheck PASS again; full 40-seed re-baseline (numbers in **Measured facts**): every forced cell without the axe byte-identical to v0.8.1 — the change is surgical; winner spread heavy 27→24; the stall alarm's worst improved 6.48→5.29s and stays red (open question 1, recorded not judged); law 3 path class 0.47% of path-fight ticks, worst 2.132px — inside the disclosed ≤6px/≤2% band. The id re-appended last (`sling, hammer, dagger, axe`) — dice weight stays uniform (one id per weapon); natural-roll assignments reshuffled into the re-baseline.

**Taste record (axe2 batch).** The batch varied head treatment, not recoloring: A twin bit + tip spike, B ringed twin bit, C chunkier with swept beards + double band, D forward head + amber pommel. D won on the first ballot with no revisions, and the sizing instruction adds a preference to the record: **the axe stands as tall as the hammer on the bot** — parity by effective length. One deviation disclosed and accepted by the pick: D's haft measures 13.5u full width at len 110 (~17.6px as played) against the universal ~11.5u shaft doctrine — chunkier; it was visible in the true-scale sheet row and chosen anyway (owner taste overrides the doctrine default; the doctrine line stands for future batches). The double-bit grammar enters **Per-family proportions**. Losers (`axe2-a/b/c.svg`) stripped at lock; `axe2-d.svg` + `axe2-sheet.png` purged at the 2026-10-18 owner cleanup (the hammer/dagger precedent).

**Step 2 (on owner go):** every weapon as a path silhouette, SVG import as the only art path, stone-vs-path audits, then the sheet follows the preview.

**Docs (2026-10-18, owner option A): the build chronicle compressed in place.** Every decision, owner quote, taste record, acceptance/calibration line and the merge ledger kept; per-step superseded baseline numbers removed (the live baseline is in **Measured facts**, law calibration history under **Core contact law**); text that stood verbatim in two places is single-sourced with pointers (interpolation mechanism → Renderer facts; launch/ranged quotes → Projectile weapon; parry hysteresis → law 2; counter-rotation rationale → law 6; the parked curved spine → Hold styles; harness roles → Testing recipe). Staleness fixed in the same pass: testing recipe (stride 13, 16 cells, ≈7 min / 170 battles), the `grandfathered` typo, retired contact-ghost wording (sling build record, hold styles, law 5), "9 ordered cells" → 16, step-entry cross-references re-pointed at this chronicle, and `measure.mjs`'s stale "3 weapons → 9 ordered combos" comments (comment-only; smoke-run after). Result: 76.9 KB → 66.3 KB — the floor for option A with everything kept; a smaller living file needs the archive split (option B), owner's call.


---

## v0.9 → v1.1 — the undocumented era (reconstructed from the code, 2026-10-19)

The owner updated the preview directly without touching the docs; this reconstruction was read out of the file's own comments and data on 2026-10-19 during the alignment session. Facts below are code-verifiable; exact dates, and any owner decisions not quoted in code comments, are UNKNOWN and marked so. No baseline was run during this era — the v0.8.3 numbers above are the last full `measure.mjs` audit that ever ran.

**v0.8.4 — the axe revision (date unknown).** The merged axe art was reworked: per its in-code comment, "double-bit, handle lost its gold pommel/collar, head scaled 1.7x, longer than the hammer (hammer len 110)". The merged blob's fills are now wood/steel/near-white only — the amber socket band and amber pommel the axe2-D taste record describes are gone, and with them the last `#c9a227` in the merged art. No ingest/gauntlet record of this revision exists.

**v0.9 — the sling became the bow (owner 2026-10-19, quote preserved in code).** "the sling should be removed, instead make it a bow, and the projectiles should be arrows." The bow inherited the sling's ranged rules (one release per revolution, spin rate = fire rate, shot carries the thrower's current dmg, spine deals no body damage but parries and swats) and is held tangentially with `tuck: false`. New: the shot leaves the grip offset outward (`off`) along the body radius — "a bow shoots where its belly faces, not along its limbs" — and the arrow draws a shaft+fletching trailing behind its head-circle hitbox (`len`). With the sling gone, no weapon carries parts data — the parts representation died here (its dead code paths stayed until v1.2). The bow's merged blob (`INGR_BOW`) matches the ingest output shape; no gauntlet record exists.

**v1.0 — the nine-weapon batch + the shot system (date unknown).** sword, spear, mace, shield, staff, wand, crossbow, boomerang, shuriken joined the roster (13 total). They were authored NOT through the ingest gate but through an in-file `build()` poly builder: weapons written as plain polygons in spine-local (d, l) units; covering circles generated from the same polygons (inscribed in a 1.2px-inflated silhouette, min r 4, dedupe 0.72 — different parameters from ingest's 1.5px/2.0, and no ε is disclosed for any of them). The in-code note: "abilities copied from the existing class: melee = axe, ranged = bow; balance later." The builder palette added gold `#d4a73a` (KG) and light blue `#9fe0ff` (KB). The shot system generalised: per-shot `kind`/`what` (orb, spark, bolt, boomerang, star), `fire: 'tip' | 'radial' | (middle)`, boomerang `turn` (curved flight, radius 200px at speed 520), shuriken `bounce: 1` (one rim ricochet — the first sanctioned exception to "shots die on the rim"). Staff and wand are RADIAL-hold launchers — the old "ranged holds tangential" class rule stopped being true here (their spines still dealt melee damage until the v1.2 fix; see below). The dagger's growth axis is +0.7 DMG per hit in this era's data — the docs had it owner-locked at +0.7 SPIN; when and why it changed is UNKNOWN (owner, 2026-10-19: "dont bother yet, the game will be balanced later on").

**v1.1 — one length for the heavy four (date unknown).** "mace, spear, hammer and axe share ONE length — the mace's tip": `LT` = maxD(INGR_MACE) ≈ 113.44 pre-scale (≈ 147.5px as played at weaponScale 1.3). Hammer and axe polys were scaled uniformly (silhouette and covering circles together) to LT; the axe's v0.8.4 "longer than the hammer" state and the old "axe len 110 = the hammer's height" instruction are both superseded. Dagger is len 78 (was 74 in the v0.8.x docs).

**Known state at the end of the era (found by the 2026-10-19 code read, before v1.2):** staff/wand spines damaged on contact (violating the owner's projectile rule — the owner had independently noticed: "i believe i caught a few with wrong behaviour"); the stone-vs-stone substep budget used `stones[0].speed`, under-budgeting mixed-speed pairs (spark 800 vs orb 430); the winner overlay hardcoded "arrows" for every shot type; the shield (tangential, no projectile) could not damage at all, making shield+shield an eternal stalemate; pure-projectile mirror cells routinely ran past 300s.

## v1.2 (2026-10-19) — the alignment session

Docs and code re-aligned after the undocumented era. Owner rulings of the day (chat, 2026-10-19): `measure.mjs` must never run without his explicit go ("dont run measure.mjs. (atleast not untill i say so, ever. ask if you want to)", "it will take too long") — a standing rule, now written into README and the Testing recipe. Dagger and shield deferred to the balance pass. The projectile rule restated and widened: "ranged weapons (shooting projectiles), their weapons do not damage, only their projetiles" — fix now. The launch-point doctrine: "when the weapon is radial, shoot from the tip, when it is the opposite, shoot from the middle, like bow and crossbow." The chronicle: "maybe remove the chronicle, its becoming too long" — moved to this archive. The art convention: "not locked, as i have not decided yet."

**Sim changes (fingerprints moved — a re-baseline is owed at the next sanctioned `measure.mjs` run):**
1. `bladeVsBody` gate is now `W.stone || W.hold === 'tangential'` — any projectile-firing weapon deals no spine damage, radial hold included (fixes staff/wand). The spine stays solid: latch + knock unchanged. The tangential clause now covers only the shield, deliberately untouched (balance-pass material).
2. The stone-vs-stone substep budget computes the worst PAIR (sA+sB over (rA+rB)/2) instead of `2·stones[0].speed` over `stones[0].r` — mixed projectile speeds had broken the documented detection guarantee.

**Verification (no `measure.mjs`):** `chromecheck.mjs` PASS. New retained probe `.qa/projectile-fix.mjs` (six forced cells × 3 seeds, ~36s): A — 0 melee hits by projectile weapons (108 melee-side hits and 592 shot landings prove it non-vacuous; 1475 spine-shove latch edges prove the shove survives); B — 0/6175 launches off the hold-derived doctrine point; C — 0/227722 stone-bearing ticks under-budgeted; D (recorded red, informational) — 3/18 battles had no winner in 300s, all pure-projectile mirror cells (bow+crossbow ×2, wand+bow ×1): pre-existing balance material for open question 1, not a v1.2 regression.

**Harness alignment (measure.mjs, edited NOT run):** law 6s rewritten from "born at the spine's middle" to the hold-derived launch doctrine (radial → tip; tangential → middle + `off` along the body radial); law 7s redefined to projectile weapons (any hold); the law-4 replica mirrors the new sim gate exactly; the parts-class law-3 line kept dormant (class empty since v0.9); forced matrix is now 169 cells × 8 seeds ≈ 1,400 battles ≈ ~1h; termination and 300s checks annotated with the expected reds (shield+shield; projectile mirrors); owner-go rule in the header. `node --check` passes on all three harnesses.

**Cosmetics/comments:** stale parts-era and sling-era comments rewritten (weapon-geometry header, fireStones header carrying the full launch doctrine, renderer header, boot-scale note, fireAcc/stones field notes); winner-overlay copy "arrows" → "shots" (generic display word — GAMES.md maps sim-internal `stone` ↔ audience-facing `shot`); the canonical filename is `spinclash-preview.html` again (the owner's working copy arrived as "spinclash-preview (3).html"; harnesses default to the canonical name).

**Docs:** GAMES.md rewritten around the 13-weapon reality (chronicle and v0.8.3 facts moved here; art conventions demoted from locked to undecided; measured-facts section states honestly that NO current baseline exists). README status updated. SPEC.md's weapon-art row unlocked (the PEBBLE frame lock is untouched — that is a separate owner decision of 2026-10-15). BUILD.md's craft checklist adjusted to the unlocked art state.

## Superseded normative sections (verbatim from the 2026-10-18 GAMES.md)

Moved here at the v1.2 alignment (2026-10-19): the sling-section and hold-styles text the shot system superseded, and the 2026-10-18 weapon-art lock the owner unlocked ("the art 'convention' is not locked, as i have not decided yet"). Owner quotes and rationale below are historical record; the CURRENT doctrine lives in GAMES.md ("Projectile weapons", "Hold styles", "Weapon art").

### Projectile weapon — the Sling (owner decisions, 2026-10-16 — BUILT in v0.7)

The roster's first ranged archetype, **named Sling and built at the owner's sign-off** ("sling it is and i confirm the rules. go", 2026-10-16 — the name and all four default rules confirmed). Three forks were put to the owner and all three were decided; the numbers below are **built and measured** (release-point history in **Hold styles**; the current numbers are the v0.7.2 baseline).

**Owner decision — blade vs projectile is a *swat*, not a parry.** A spinning blade touching an enemy projectile destroys the projectile and nothing else happens: no parry event, **no spin flip on either fighter**. This is the named, documented exception that rule 5 always reserved for weapon traits — rule 5's recoil keeps applying to blade-vs-blade exactly as written, and the counter-rotation invariant (rules 5 + 6) stays whole because a projectile has no spin to flip. The exception must be readable where the weapon is taught (reveal card / legend line), per rule 5's own caveat.

**Owner decision — firing is a tip release per revolution.** One projectile leaves the weapon tip per full revolution of the spin, so **spin rate is fire rate** and the existing growth language carries the new axis for free: `+SPD / Hit` on this weapon *is* faster shooting. No cooldown stat, no ammo, no new vocabulary — which also keeps open question 3 (skill vs ability model) unresolved rather than silently answering it. Firing must fall out of the physics because nobody pilots these bots.

**Owner decision — prototype now, before the balance pass.** Build the third weapon first, then run one balance pass over the full roster (then 9 ordered combos instead of 4; the roster has since grown to 16 cells). Rationale recorded at the time: rebalancing twice is worse than once, and ranged pressure is a candidate answer to the hammer-orbit stall that open question 1 is chasing.

**How the contact law extends.** Rules 1, 4 and 6 carry over untouched (a projectile touching a body is a damaging hit; it is consumed by that hit, so one-damage-per-touch is automatic; opening spins are unaffected). Rule 2 gains the swat clause above. Rule 3 becomes asymmetric: a projectile has no body behind it to push, so instead of being separated it **loses every collision it makes** — destroyed by a blade (swat), destroyed by a body (after dealing damage), never overlapping either on a rendered frame. Detection keeps the same guarantee as blades: substeps sized so relative travel per substep ≤ half the narrowest contact window, now including projectile speed against blade-tip speed (the largest relative velocity in the sim — this is the real engineering cost, not the drawing).

**The four default rules — owner-confirmed 2026-10-16, built as written:** stone-vs-stone cancels both (symmetric, provable) · stones die on the rim, since rule 1 says walls never damage · no friendly fire · the stone is drawn as a disc whose circle *is* its hitbox, so "what you see is what hits" survives untouched. **Implementation note on friendly fire:** it is built as *owner-blindness* — a stone is never tested against its owner's body or blade, so it spawns at the pouch end (visually the correct release point) and can never self-destruct or self-swat.

**Stone damage (owner decision, 2026-10-16):** the stone launches with the thrower's **current** damage — snapshotted at launch — and there is no separate projectile stat (one damage number per fighter, `a.dmg`; it grows on hit via the fighter's skill, so a Heavy's stones fatten +0.9 per hit while a Speedy's stay light and come faster). Under the uniform base stats decision the Sling's built numbers are ω 3.5 / melee dmg 5 / len 62 / +0.7 spin per hit (spin rate = fire rate; base cadence one stone per ~1.8s). Stone: radius 6px, speed 620 px/s, straight flight (no gravity), tangential release in the spin direction **from the weapon's middle — the spine's t = 0.5 point, the grip on the rim** (v0.7.2 ranged class rule, owner 2026-10-17: "i want it to shoot from the middle"; the v0.7/v0.7.1 pouch-end release is history). The drawn pouch/stone disc still sits near the t = 1 end — a known visual mismatch the parked redesign owns (owner: "it doesnt, matter for now, just leave it. we will do a whole redesign of all weapons later"). The cord and pouch stay a **normal solid under all six contact laws** — parries weapon-vs-weapon, swats stones, and body contact keeps the latch + knock shove — but per the **v0.7.2 ranged class rule** (owner 2026-10-17: "ranged weapons dont do damage anymore, only their projectiels does, but the weapon can parry still like normal weapon vs weapon colissions") the spine deals **no body damage**: the stone is the Sling's only damage, and its +0.7 spin growth now fires only on landings.

**Build record (v0.7 2026-10-16 · v0.7.2 2026-10-17).** Firing is physics, not a scheduler: a per-fighter radian accumulator (`fireAcc += |spin|·sdt`) releases one stone per 2π — growth on the spin axis *is* fire-rate growth, no new vocabulary. Substep sizing gained a projectile budget term (stone speed + foe tip speed + impulse margin against half the narrowest stone window — the swat window); the audit checks the sim's chosen k against both budgets every tick. Stones are killed on contact and never resolved (law 3's asymmetry), in a fixed deterministic order per substep: rim → body → swat → stone-stone. Copy locked: reveal line `a stone per rev · blades swat stones`, legend brief `+SPD / Hit · stone / rev`, winner-overlay stone tail only when a sling fought. The v0.7 swat/landing contact-pose ghosts were retired with all ghosts at step1d — the swat now reads purely as the stone popping. **Palette note (owner veto point):** the early sketch said "leather cord (brown)"; the locked frame's six-color economy has no brown, so the cord drew in the dark `edge` material — resolved by the house palette (cord = dark wood or ink, decided in the sling batch). **v0.7.2:** release moved to the spine middle (`spineFrame` t = 0.5; the firing pose rides on every `stoneFire`); `bladeVsBody` latches and shoves (re-arm + knock) then returns damage-free for tangential-hold attackers — no HP, no growth, no event; laws 6s/7s and the law-4 ranged exception added, all mutation-tested at birth; sling-free cells came out bit-identical to v0.7.1.

**Audit extension (`measure.mjs`, mutation-tested).** Seven projectile-law checks, each proven to have teeth by a throwaway mutation (disabled swat → 3s FAIL; 20px ranged stone damage → 1s FAIL; silent rim death → 4s FAIL; swat flipping spin → core law 5 FAIL; launch reverted to the t = 1 end → 6s FAIL, worst offset exactly 31px = half the chord; ranged spine damaging again → 7s FAIL **and** core law 4 FAIL — hits with no paired edge):
- **1s** stone damage only on stone-touches-body — every landing recomputes to contact from its stored death pose vs the victim (0 phantom ever);
- **2s** swats only on exact stone-blade touch, recomputed from the stored swatter pose (0 without touch ever); that swats flip *no* spin is enforced by core law 5's sign audit, not a separate check;
- **3s** stones never overlap foe blade, foe body, rim, or another stone at tick-entry (rendered) poses — and because a stone dies on its first contact substep, an entry-frame overlap would also prove a missed kill, so this doubles as the anti-teleport bracket ("outside at entry, biting at death");
- **4s** accounting — every fired stone has exactly one proven departure (landed / swatted / rim / cross / cleared at the round roll) or is still in flight at the battle end: 0 lost, 0 orphans, 0 double-departures ever. A stone vanishing without an event is the tunneling alarm;
- **5s** the substep budget audit — 0 violations, 0 `maxSub` engagements;
- **6s** mid-spine launch — every `stoneFire` carries its firing pose and recomputes to spine t = 0.5 (0 off-center ever);
- **7s** ranged = no melee — zero non-stone hits by tangential-hold spines; the core law-4 replica knows the exception (a ranged rising edge is a flag-audited zero-damage shove with no hit event to pair).
Stone hits do NOT pair with the bladeStuck replica (a stone is consumed — law 4 is structural) and deliberately do not feed the law-5 parry magnitude pairing (a stone hit grows the thrower *after* the same substep's parry, unlike blade hits — a pairing bug this caught in dev). Stall-timer semantics widened: hit/parry/**swat**/bounce; event-rate includes stone events. The combo matrix is **forced** (curator knob) — now 16 ordered cells × 8 shared seeds (the roster has been 4 weapons since step1), because natural rolls thin to ~10 rounds per cell.

### Hold styles — radial melee, tangential ranged (owner decision, 2026-10-16 — BUILT in v0.7.1)

How a weapon is held on the body. Owner verdict on the sling's radial grip: **"it should not point outwards, it should be held along the edge of the body"** — the redesign was agreed fork by fork (the hitbox follows the drawing; the grip sits centered on the rim, bow-style) and built at the owner's "go". It is a **class rule for every future weapon**: melee holds **radial** (spine straight out from the body centre, grip tucked under the disc — unchanged since the perception pass); ranged holds **tangential** along the rim.

**The tangential placement (built).** The spine is a **straight tangent chord** at the body's rim point: grip centered on the rim at t = 0.5, both ends floating ~7px clear of the disc (sling len 62 → ends at √(64²+31²) ≈ 71px from centre). The sling's parts were re-profiled two-sided around the grip: tail knot at t = 0, dark `edge` cord on both sides (the brown veto stands), pouch + stone disc drawn near the t = 1 end. **Throwing convention (v0.7.2): a projectile always leaves the spine's middle — t = 0.5, the grip point on the rim — tangentially in the current spin direction** (the v0.7/v0.7.1 t = 1-end release is history; the drawn pouch placement is a known visual mismatch the parked redesign owns). **Ranged damage rule (same build): a ranged spine never damages the body** — it stays solid (latch + knock shove), parries weapon-vs-weapon and swats stones exactly like a melee weapon, but its projectile is its only damage. Both are class rules for every future ranged weapon; both owner quotes are recorded in **Projectile weapon**. The grip capsule straddles the rim line — its inner half draws under the body disc, the same tuck the radial grip has. Trail arcs are retired channel-wide (step1c), so the chord needs no trail exception. A curved rim-hugging spine remains **parked**: the path representation could carry it now, but nothing needs it yet — the straight chord is the mechanics truth until the sling batch decides otherwise.

**Why the contact law held by construction.** Placement lives in **one function** (`spineFrame`: spine origin, unit direction, span for a body pose) consumed identically by the collision circles, the renderer and the harness's audit recomputes — the drawing cannot drift from the hitbox ("what you see is what hits" survives the redesign structurally, not by discipline). Radial placement is numerically unchanged (probe: ≤1e-13px against the old closed form), so hammer and dagger silhouettes are pixel-identical. The v0.7.2 re-baseline re-proved all six core laws and all seven projectile laws green; the mid-launch and no-melee-damage rules were each mutation-tested at birth (laws 6s/7s), and law 6s recomputes every launch from the pose stored on its `stoneFire` event, so a stone can never be born anywhere but the spine's middle.

**Measured consequences (v0.7.1, recorded — not judged;** balance owner-deferred; the sling deliberately loses outward melee reach — rim-height chord, extent ~77px vs ~132px before): battle median 80.3s → 114.3s; sling cells moved toward Heavy (sling+hammer speedy 8/11 → 1/16); stone landings 11.2% → 8.7% of throws, rim deaths 81.1% → 84.2%, crosses 16 → 58. The stall alarm class survives and is no longer dagger-only: worst 6.02s (seed 12).

**v0.7.2 moved the numbers again (recorded — not judged;** mid-spine launch + no ranged melee damage): battle median 114.3s → 136.8s; **every mixed sling cell flipped to the non-sling side** — with a spine that can't damage, the sling's only offence is an ~8%-landing stone; the sling+sling mirror became the longest cell (50.9s → 118.3s); stall worst 6.02s → 7.48s (seed 12 again). Superseded by the current baseline in **Measured facts**; all frozen until the balance pass (open question 1).


## Weapon art conventions (locked 2026-10-18 — living lock)

The channel's weapon language, consolidated from the step1e–1k decisions so it reads as one rule set instead of a dozen chronology entries. **Locked but livable: this is new for us; amendments happen by owner decision and get recorded as amendments (owner, 2026-10-18: "we might edit it later, as this is still new for us").** Reference implementations outrank prose: the merged data constants in `spinclash-preview.html` (`INGR_HAMMER`, `INGR_DAGGER`, `INGR_AXE`) are the sources of record — the hammer/dagger source SVGs were purged at the 2026-10-18 owner cleanup, and the owner-paste `AXE_POLY` left the preview at v0.8.3 (the axe2-D source SVG + contact sheet were purged at the same cleanup on the owner's word); the sling joins when its batch locks. Physics disclosures (notch-fill, epsilon, pin band) stay in the build chronicle and testing recipe — they are collision truth, not art direction.

**Family look.** Flat fills; one uniform ink outline (#2e2b22, stroke-width 8 per 1000 source units, counted as body by ingest); no gradients, no texture, no glow. Palette locked to five: wood #7a4a2a, dark wood #4f3220 (butts, grips), amber #c9a227 (bands, sockets, guards, pommels), steel #8fa3b5 (blades, heads), near-white #eaf6ff reserved strictly for cutting-edge and striking-face shapes — and optional per weapon (the hammer's white-face veto, step1f, is the precedent). One family across the whole channel; a weapon that cannot sit in this family is not a weapon for this channel.

**The shaft doctrine.** Universal shaft width: ~11.5 units full width at base scale (~15 as played at weaponScale 1.3) for every hafted weapon — the hammer v4 width the owner approved. The shaft is plain wood from body to guard with zero detail; the butt tucks invisible under the bot disc (the render clips the own-body circle out of the weapon pass); handle-less weapons (shuriken class) carry `tuck: false` or the clip erases them. All handle detail lives from the guard outwards; the visible haft (rim to guard) earns at most nothing — the owner rejected even a single band near the body (step1h).

**Per-family proportions.** Hammer: lateral rectangle head — wider across the shaft than along it (≥ ~1.4:1), double flat striking faces, chamfered corners, head starting around 60% of total length because the head is massive (v5, step1k; the near-square v4 head read "vertical" at half the rotations and failed the owner's eye). Dagger: stub shaft (butt + shaft ~17% of total, only a bit visible beyond the disc), stocky blade 60–75% of a short total. Sword (future): longer total, normal shaft, slimmer blade — differentiation from the dagger carries via total length and stoutness, not a blade-share cap. Axe (axe2-D, owner pick 2026-10-18): forward double-bit head starting around 60% of total length — opposed crescent bits (convex cutting arcs, concave backs landing on the steel slab), bright near-white slivers along the cutting arcs, amber socket band behind the slab, plain haft, amber pommel at the butt just outside the rim; sized to the hammer (len 110 — owner: "make it the same height as hammer on the bots"). The owner-paste grammar (single-bit crescent, open rings that stay real holes) retired with the v2 art at v0.8.3. Sling (when its batch locks): pouch centered on the stone launch point, chunky plain cords, stone disc as focal point; cord width provisional until then.

**Legibility.** Squint test at 120 pixels long: the weapon must still read as itself. Minimum feature 15 source units per 1000. Silhouette-first: variants in a batch differ in shape, not recoloring. Weapons draw above every body (step1c). Exaggeration welcome — oversized heads read at fight scale, realism does not.

**Process.** The assistant is the sole supplier, the owner the sole selector: batches of four-plus candidates as standalone SVGs plus one contact sheet; the owner picks by letter or redirects; the pick passes `ingest.mjs` (validation + covering + combat gauntlet against the whole roster) before merge; losers stripped at lock; the pick's proportions and the owner's revision notes enter the taste record (the step-1 chronicle entry) so later candidates start closer. External art taps are closed (owner decision, step1e); supplier art gets no privilege at the gate.

**Provisional, not locked.** `weaponScale` 1.3 — owner judges it against a fuller roster; one number to turn, ~6 min re-baseline per turn. Sling cord width. Anything the first sword batch teaches about the dagger/sword doctrine.

Both are verbatim.

The corrected metric (raster + exact-EDT union SDF), the true numbers, the owner directive and the CIRCLES_V2 fix live in the next entry, which supersedes this one. Still valid from it: the hammer finding it confirmed (+13.6px poke, killing circle d=118.1 l=−52.7 r=13.1 — the svg stays as its evidence) and the Part-A drift measurement (victim freezes AT contact; attacker leftover kill-tick swing ≤2.98px / 0.013 rad) — the sub-3px the kill still owns.]

### Retired at the round-4 trim (2026-10-23, owner order: "check if the docs align with everything, trim parts")

Two blocks retired verbatim: the re-presentation glint saga (two paragraphs of the 2026-10-22 edge-leading entry — the mechanism was removed the same day, superseded by the flip transition; the tombstone in GAMES.md points here) and the superseded current-state bullet announcing the nunchaku commission as paste-ready (batch-4 delivered against current canonical the same day; the `.qa/base-pre-cleanup.html` snapshot it references was purged at round 3).

**Re-presentation glint (same day, owner option 2 after the flip's playtest — "good its working, but i was noticing it is like snapping instantly, can we make liek a short very short transition of some sort, if not then im fine with it also"; built at his pick "2, because at some point we are probably gonan have similar weapons and not only katana and scythe" — generic for ANY `lead` weapon by design).** The geometric transition was rejected at proposal (the owner chose the compliant option from the menu): a squash tween would draw a silhouette that is not the collision shape for ~2–4 frames per flagged parry — re-opening the gap-fire/visible-miss classes the visible-contact-first directive exists to kill — and tweening the SIM geometry would thin both blades at every clang (a recurring pass-through window: the tunneling class, hard no). Shipped mechanism — renderer-only luminance: `consumeEvents` arms `glintT[idx] = CFG.glintMs` (70, a wall-ms dial) for each fighter whose entry weapon carries `lead` (a = fighters[0] by bladeVsBlade's construction); `draw()` burns it down linearly on the body flash's own wall clock (dtFrames → 0 under a hitstop, so it freezes with the frame; `resetFx` clears per round); `drawGlint` paints the weapon's near-white KP polys — the palette's cutting/striking face, i.e. the business contour — in the flash white at decaying alpha (0.9·α, the body-flash strength recipe), through the LIVE mirrored placement (the same `polyPath` + fp as the art pass, same tuck clip): luminance over truth by construction. The dead never glint; unflagged weapons never glint (the flag IS the trigger — future edge-leaders inherit the glint with zero code, the owner's stated reason for option 2). Proofs: `.qa/glintproof.mjs` (permanent; DOM-stub frame loop + a recording ctx on the main canvas) ALL PASS — trigger non-vacuous both directions (s11: 11 flagged-side parries; control s7: 15 parries, none flagged); B1 arm ≤ glintMs on the flagged fighter ONLY (53.3/70 first observed frame, scythe side, mace side dark); B2 linear burn to 0 in 4 frames; **B3 the contract check: every glint fill (48 fills, 576 points) is a strict subset of the same frame's art-pass points — the glint can never draw a contour the mirrored art did not (0 off-art)**; B4 the control battle never glints and logs zero white poly fills; B5 reveal frames carry [0,0] including the reveal after an arm. Fingerprints **8/8 byte-identical** (renderer-only by construction — `glintT` is never read by the sim; `.qa/fp-after.json` untouched, no re-ritual). Suite: chromecheck PASS · leadproof ALL PASS · inscription gate PASS · killstill 30/30 · hitstill 525/525 · reveal-probe ALL PASS, zero edits · projectile-fix PROBE PASS (D 4/18 recorded). `info().glint` exposed for probes. Dial for the owner's eye: `CFG.glintMs`. **Visibility fix (same day, owner playtest: "i dont see it at all, it looks like just before with instant snapping"):** the first pass painted `#fff` over ONLY the near-white KP contour — white on `#eaf6ff` is zero contrast at fight scale (the body flash reads because it is white over a saturated cast color). The birth proof had verified the timing, the trigger scoping and the geometry, but not the perceptual strength — a gap no harness measures; the owner's eye caught it (the house loop working as designed). Fix: `drawGlint` now flashes the flagged weapon's FULL silhouette (every poly) in the body-flash white at 0.9·α — the hit-flash's own grammar, on the blade that just rebounded — and `CFG.glintMs` rose 70 → 90 (still "very short"; the dial stands). Re-run: glintproof ALL PASS (B1 arm 73.3/90 flagged-side only, B2 burns to 0 in 5 frames, B3 2478 points across 295 fills all ⊆ the same frame's art pass, 0 off-art, B4/B5 clean) · fingerprints 8/8 byte-identical (renderer-only holds) · chromecheck PASS. Recorded lesson for future FX: a luminance effect needs a contrast check against its own ground color, or it ships invisible. **Glint r2 (same day, owner retest: "i still dont see anything, same as before"):** two changes. (1) The 90ms linear decay was still a flicker lost inside the clang's motion — the envelope is reshaped: SOLID white (0.95α) held for the first ~45% of the burn, then a linear ramp out; `CFG.glintMs` 90 → 140 (still far under the body flash's ~370ms visible tail; the dial stands). (2) The **build stamp** was introduced: a static `build glint-r2` in the page header line and the `<title>` (tooling chrome — outside the canvas, never recorded into a frame). It settles the stale-file ambiguity at one glance and **bumps on every owner-visible change from here on** (recorded convention; the assistant owns it).

- **Canonical file cleaned; the nunchaku commission is paste-ready (2026-10-22).** A whole-file code-review pass at owner order removed the dead code and smells it found (a boot-throw message bug, a misfiled `nudgeTick` init, a dead parameter, a self-cancelling expression, per-substep allocations in the resolver's hot path) — behaviour identical, fingerprints byte-identical, baseline untouched (**Measured facts**, last entry). The pre-cleanup canonical is kept at `.qa/base-pre-cleanup.html`: if the nunchaku brief was pasted to the artist BEFORE the cleanup, the delivery diffs against that snapshot and its art hunks lift into the current file.

### Retired at the 2026-10-23 hygiene pass — the 2026-10-19 era (owner pick: retire the twelve oldest Measured-facts entries verbatim; the tombstone with their permanent outcomes is in GAMES.md's Measured facts)

**v1.2 baseline, part 0 — natural rolls + determinism (2026-10-19, `.qa/baseline-v1.2-part0.txt`).** The first sanctioned `measure.mjs` run of the v1.x era, on the new parallel/tiered harness (`--quick`, 2 jobs, ~3 min): 42 battles · 102 rounds · 1624 hits · 1817 parries · 6,392,749 substep poses. **Determinism PASS** (seed 1 identical across two processes) and **all 13 law checks PASS** — 0 phantom hits, 0 slack parries, law-3 path band worst **1.839px** on 2205/714575 path-fight ticks (0.31%, inside the disclosed ≤6px/≤2%), replica + signed-spin laws clean, all seven shot laws clean including the new hold-derived launch doctrine and the worst-pair budget. Stats: termination **39/40** (one natural roll exhausted the 420s budget) · winner spread **speedy 15 / heavy 24** · 23 two-round / 16 three-round · battle median **123.0s**, mean 149.2s, max 420.0s, ≥60s in 38/40 · round median 38.7s, max 353.9s · hits 16.1/round, parries 19.6/round, events **5.6/s** · stall median 3.65s, p90 5.71s, **worst 7.50s — FAIL vs the 5s line** (v0.8.3's red was 5.29s) · max spin median 11.5, max 23.5, **buzzsaw 0/40** · max dmg 19. Shot ledger: **7919 thrown · 828 landed (10.5%) · 491 swatted (6.2%) · 6125 rim (77.3%) · 362 crossed · 79 cleared · 0 unaccounted.** Reds are the deferred-balance trio (open question 1): termination, stall, battle length. The v0.8.3 full baseline (4-weapon roster) remains in `CHRONICLE-archive.md`.

**Fast-probe re-run post-CIRCLES_V2 (2026-10-19, `.qa/projectile-fix-postcircles.txt`) — the first run on the regenerated circles.** Projectile rule, launch doctrine and the mixed-speed budget stay zero-violation: A — 0 melee hits by projectile weapons, non-vacuous (601 landings, 1452 spine-shove latch edges, 108 melee-side hits); B — 0/6760 launches off the doctrine point; C — 0/245,083 under-provisioned budget ticks. The informational termination red moved 3/18 → **7/18** (bow+crossbow s1/s3, staff+wand s1–s3, wand+bow s1/s2 — every red a pure-projectile mirror; the mixed cells all terminate, 55–142s). Wall now ~100–175s, was ~36s at birth (substep cost rose where MINR fell — the inscribe entry's known watch item). Feeds open question 1.

**Solidity suite — first run (2026-10-19, `--solidity`, `.qa/solidity-run1.txt`).** The owner's tunneling/overlap tier, built and validated the same day: 40 naturals + the 12-cell overlap gauntlet × 3 seeds = 76 battles, **7m18s wall** at 2 sandbox jobs (~2–3 min on 6–8 cores) — inside the owner's 10-minute target. **All 13 law checks PASS.** Headline solidity numbers: law 3 (path) worst **5.568px** on 4328/1,127,184 path-fight ticks (0.38%) — the deepest pin transient recorded to date (v0.8.3 saw 2.132px; part 0's naturals 1.839px), inside the disclosed ≤6px/≤2% band but with shrinking headroom; recorded, not judged — if a future run crosses 6px, the resolver's pin escape or the band's attribution gets revisited. Law 3s: **0 shot overlaps over 519,534 stone-bearing frames.** Law 4s: 12,192 shots thrown, 0 lost / 0 orphans / 0 double-departures. Law 5s: 0 budget violations, 0 maxSub engagements. All 36 gauntlet battles terminated (max 312.7s; wand+bow and staff+crossbow medians ~97–107s at 3 seeds — the mirror-length red is seed-dependent). Naturals' three reds unchanged (39/40, stall worst 7.50s, max 420.0s). Post-run report fix: the matrix header printed the full-tier "8 seeds/cell" constant — now mode-aware; the run file keeps its original text.

**Diagnosis record (2026-10-19, retained probes):** `.qa/natscan.mjs` — every natural seed 11–40 is healthy in isolation (30 battles in 72s, worst single battle 7.0s, all terminate; max k 29, max |spin| 23.5, max 9 shots in flight). `.qa/boomdiag.mjs` — **the boomerang accumulation fear is disproven**: boomerangs die reliably (of 181 thrown: 41 landed, 23 swatted, 36 crossed, 74 rim), in-flight stays ≤ 5, the mirror terminates (~83s). `.qa/fingerprint.mjs` — the v1.2 refactor was proven sim-neutral (8 battle fingerprints byte-identical before/after). The "stalled" baseline runs were sandbox process-reaping, **not** a game or harness pathology — the sim itself never showed a slow battle.

**Reveal +1s tune — sim-timeline fingerprint re-baseline (2026-10-19, `.qa/fp-after.json`; pre-change bytes were retained at `.qa/fp-pre-rt285.json`, byte-identical to the old v1.2 reference — file purged in the 2026-10-22 cleanup, the delta proof below stands).** The owner's reveal extension (`revealTicks` 165 → 285, `land` 0.40/0.58 → 0.32/0.46) gates `startFight`, so every fingerprint battle shifts — a sim-timeline change, not renderer-only. The delta proof bounds it exactly: all 8 battles grew by precisely **120 ticks × rounds** (2-round battles +240, the 3-round hammer+dagger +360) or held at the 150 s cap — battle dynamics, rolls and outcomes untouched. `reveal-probe` ALL PASS with **zero edits** (it parses `revealTicks`/`VS` from the file — new landings tick ~91/~131, pre-hold exactly 7, last shuffle steps 10/12 ticks, exactly 285 drawn frames per reveal); `chromecheck` PASS after its stub-frame advance went 150 → 250 (the fight-clock guard needs ≥100 fight ticks past the longer reveal).

**Simultaneous weapon landings (2026-10-19) — renderer-only, no fingerprint movement.** Owner: "i want oth weapons to appear at the same time" → `VS.land` [0.32, 0.46] → [0.39, 0.39] — the stagger's midpoint, so both slams hit at tick ~111 of 285 and the end hold grows to ~1.45s. Both sides now share one roll window (~96 ticks); the closed-form cadence math keeps the last shuffle steps ≈ 12/10 ticks, well over the probe's 3-tick deceleration floor. The sim never reads `VS`, so fingerprints are unchanged by construction — no delta proof, no re-baseline. The `reveal-probe` stagger assertion was flipped to a simultaneity assertion in the same edit; per owner word this session ("dont run all these harnesses and mjs this time, it takes so long") **no harness was run** — `chromecheck` is unaffected (it never reads `VS`).

**Hitstop (2026-10-19) — frame-loop layer, no fingerprint movement.** The freeze-on-damage consultation was sanctioned with trigger policy 1 (owner: "implement it, and do 1 trigger policy"): every damage hit = a 60ms freeze, the killing blow = 400ms (`CFG.hitStopMs` / `CFG.koStopMs`), expressed in sim ticks and counted on the playback clock (fast-forward shortens, slow-mo stretches), max-merged per tick, with the wall-clock FX (flash, banner) frozen so the impact frame holds. Built as the `stopTicks` accumulator gate in `frame()`, armed by `consumeEvents` on `hit` events (`kill` picks the tier) — the sim cannot tick during a freeze. Proofs: `chromecheck` PASS with a new hitstop guard (over the battle-to-end run `stopMs > 0` was seen and `G.t` NEVER advanced while a freeze was in flight); `.qa/fingerprint.mjs` → `.qa/fp-hitstop.json` **byte-identical** to the `.qa/fp-after.json` reference (direct-tick harnesses never touch the gate — identity was the expectation, now it is the record).

**Parry-push tune — the parry-loop fix, option 1 (2026-10-19, `parryPush` 150 → 525 = 3.5×; owner pick from the numbered options: "1"; pre-change bytes retained at `.qa/fp-pre-pp525.json`, `.qa/fp-after.json` re-baselined).** Diagnosis: the point-blank machine-gun parries were a hysteresis artifact — at body contact the blades re-touched on every `parryPad` latch reset, each fresh touch flipped both spins (law 5) and added a 150 shove too weak to end the episode (compare the floor trampoline's 1050), and the bottom-centre cradle (curved wall + gravity) kept feeding the pair back in; with zero on-screen punctuation the buzz read as engine seizure, and it was dead time besides. The fix is the magnitude dial only — the laws fix the shove *direction* (body-centre line) and the trigger (exact touch), neither moved, no law text amended. Sim-timeline change: all 8 fingerprint cells drifted, as required. Delta proof on the same cells (parries / ticks): the worst loop cell hammer+dagger s5 went **53 → 24 parries** and ended **6419 ticks sooner** (15901 → 9482); natural s11 **17 → 8**; totals 229 → 187. Two cells ticked slightly up (staff+hammer 17 → 22, shield+hammer 21 → 25) — timeline divergence past the first parry, not re-buzz; both still terminated far under cap. `chromecheck` PASS; `reveal-probe` ALL PASS with zero edits (it parses `revealTicks`/`VS`, untouched). The owner's planned "more bounce" feel tune is independent of this fix.

**K.O. kill still (2026-10-19, owner pick "1") — renderer-only, no fingerprint movement.** The frozen-K.O.-frame consultation (owner, after the seed-63230 screenshot: "is it hard to make it touch or ? dont code yet") ended at his option pick "1": the renderer latches the contact poses every kill `hit` event already carries (`x,y` victim · `ax,ay,aAng,aW` blade killer · `sx,sy,sr` + last-drawn flight state via `lastShots` for projectile kills) and draws the whole `ko` phase and the `over` freeze at that contact truth instead of the tick-end pose — a `view`-override, so art, weapon and hitbox debug stay in agreement; the latch drops at the next reveal; `info().koStill` exposes it to probes. Proofs: new `.qa/killstill.mjs` (seconds) — Part A over 14 seeds / 22 kills (15 blade, 7 stone): gContact ≤ 0.3 at **22/22** latched poses; Part B DOM-stub frame loop on seed 63230: latch bit-equal to the kill event's poses, stable across 77 ko frames, dropped at round 2's reveal. `chromecheck` PASS; fingerprint → `.qa/fp-killstill.json` **byte-identical** to `.qa/fp-after.json`; `reveal-probe` ALL PASS with zero edits.

**The inscribe fix — CIRCLES_V2: every weapon's covering circles regenerated union-inscribed in the drawn ink (2026-10-19, owner directive; sim-data change, fingerprints re-baselined).** The owner turned the diagnosis into a standing priority: "well that is very bad for the viewers, they dont wanna see these 'gap' hits, this should be a top priority to always make weapons visibly touch something else before something happens, like nobody wants to see parry fire or body taking damage when there is a visible gap between etc" — implemented as option 1 (regen all + permanent gate). Corrected ground truth first, via raster + exact-EDT union signed distance (now the permanent gate `.qa/inscribe.mjs`, ~6s): **poke** (circle past own ink) — hammer +13.6px (exactly the seed-63230 killing circle d=118.1 l=−52.7 r=13.1, confirmed), axe +8.5, bow +2.0, dagger +1.5 (the four INGR bakes were the true pokers), cover() batch +0.10..+0.26 only (its 1.2u margin scales ×1.3 weaponScale to 1.56px against the 1.5px ink half-stroke — the old "staff 14.1 / spear 13.7 / mace 13.2" readings were metric artifacts).
**Miss** (ink outside every circle — visible contact that fires nothing; a second doctrine violation nobody had measured): every weapon, up to **13.1px** (crossbow limb strip + sight pin, shuriken spikes — cover()'s min-r-4 skipped thin paint entirely). Fix: `CIRCLES_V2` baked into the file (WORLD px, applied after the weaponScale loop; the four INGR JSON `circles` arrays emptied; runtime `cover()`/`segD` deleted), generated by `.qa/inscribe.mjs --regen` — the ingest.mjs house greedy maximal-inscribed packing (largest-circle-first over uncovered centres, full-radius coverage marking, prune) rebuilt on the EDT union SDF, margin 1.2px (circles ≥0.3px inside the ink ⇒ poke ≤ 0 by construction), candidates hill-climbed onto thin features' medial axes, RMIN 2.0 (MINR sizes the parry substep budget — 2.0 keeps worst-case pairs at the INGR weapons' current cost). Gate numbers after: **poke −0.30..−0.33px all 13; miss ≤ 4.39px** (axe 4.30, crossbow 4.39, dagger 4.05 — tapered tips + hairline paint, disclosed; was 13.14); n = 13..69 circles/weapon (was 9..75); MINR 2.0..2.8 everywhere (was 1.25..6.76).
Sim delta (`fp-pre-inscribe.json` → `fp-post-inscribe.json`; all 8 cells changed, as expected for a reach change): hammer+dagger s5 9482→9388, staff+hammer s1 7634→7946, boomerang+shuriken s1 12228→17098 (battles decided by phantom reach now last longer), shield+hammer s4 9082→11038, natural s11 9262→7816; bow+crossbow s2 / wand+bow s3 / natural s7 stay at the 18000 cap. Suite: chromecheck PASS (~4.4s, unchanged), killstill ALL PASS (22 kills / 14 seeds, gContact ≤ 0.3 on 22/22 — with the circles ≥0.3px inside the weapon ink and the disc ink 2px outside bodyR, every K.O. freeze now shows ink overlap of ≥ ~1.7px), reveal-probe ALL PASS (reveal untouched), inscription gate PASS. `.qa/fp-after.json` re-baselined := post bytes; pre retained at `.qa/fp-pre-inscribe.json`; the bake surgery is preserved at `.qa/bake-circles-v2.mjs`. Cost note: substeps rose where MINR fell (bow+crossbow fingerprint cell 3.3s → 10.3s in node; several battles shortened instead) — a watch item for the balance/perf pass. **Same directive, projectile side (renderer-only, fingerprints byte-identical — proven):** spark/star/boom/bolt inks did NOT cover their hitbox discs (star/spark notches leave up to ~5px of visible gap facing the victim; boom's banana and the bolt's flanks likewise) — every kind now gets the orb's halo treatment:
a faint full-disc fill at exactly radius r under the art (`HALO` alphas are dials: spark 0.22 gold, star 0.24 / bolt 0.18 / boom 0.18 steel). **Standing rule: `node .qa/inscribe.mjs` after ANY art/poly/weaponScale edit and for every new weapon** (FAIL on poke > 0.12px or miss > 4.6px; --regen re-bakes). The kill still itself stays (owner never said `r`).

**Code-review pass (2026-10-19 — renderer + boot only, fingerprints byte-identical and proven: `.qa/fp-pre-review.json` = `.qa/fp-post-review.json` = `.qa/fp-after.json`).** Whole-file audit for bugs, dead code, duplication and doctrine gaps. Fixed: (1) **the bow's arrow was the only shot whose hitbox disc was not inked** — the halo pass keyed on `stone.kind` and the bow is kindless, so its arrows bypassed `drawShot` and drew bare disc flanks (to ~1.6px at r 6.5) while the crossbow bolt — the identical `drawArrow` art — got its halo; every shot now routes through `drawShot`, and `HALO` gains `orb` 0.28 (folded in from its inline copy) and `arrow` steel 0.18 — the renderer-facts sentence "every kind inks its own hitbox disc" is now true structurally, and the owner's visible-contact-first directive covers the bow's only damage source. (2) **The K.O. still's never-drawn fallback was invisible**: `consumeEvents` read `WEAPONS[id].kind` (no such field — kind lives on `stone`), so the fallback always got `'stone'`, a kind `drawShot` had no branch for; a shot fired AND landed inside one tick drew nothing on the freeze frame. It now draws the documented "plain stone at the impact point" (generic steel disc at exactly r) and the guessed-flight-state guards (`vx || 1` etc.) retired with it. (3) Dead code removed: consumeEvents' `ko` flash branch (the killing `hit` already flashed the loser — same tick, earlier in the array; only hits write HP), `TH.card.sub`, both boot-time covering-circle scaling loops (every `circles` array is empty pre-CIRCLES_V2 assignment — provably no-op), `drawWeaponOf`'s unreachable dead-fighter alpha ternary, the duplicate `fightPop`/`pop` pair. (4) Single-sourced: `SKILL_SHORT` derives from `FIGHTERS[].skill`, the frame-loop playback rates derive from `CFG.tickHz`, and the bow's stone carries its own `what: 'arrow'` (was living off the meta fallback). **Reported, NOT changed (a sim-data change — ritual + owner go) — RESOLVED 2026-10-21 at owner go; the fix and its proofs live in the REACH-fix entry below:** the radial-hold `REACH` term inflates the 1.5px ink half-stroke laterally inside the hypotenuse (`hypot(d, |l|+1.5)`) instead of radially outside it (`hypot(d,l)+1.5` — what the tangential branch already does), so REACH can under-state the true circle extent by ≤ ~1.2–1.5px on tip-most vertices. Benign as shipped: the parryGap prune keeps ≥1.6px of slack (`parryPad` 4 vs the ≤2×1.2px shortfall) and the substep budget spends ≤2.5% of its half-window margin (both baselines green) — but it is a latent tightness, recorded so it is never "discovered" as new. Suite on the reviewed file: chromecheck PASS · inscription gate PASS (poke −0.30..−0.33, miss ≤4.39) · killstill ALL PASS (22 kills / 14 seeds, gContact ≤0.3 on 22/22) · reveal-probe ALL PASS · projectile-fix PROBE PASS (re-run entry above).
