### Retired from GAMES.md L515 (2026-10-28)

**The first sanctioned `measure.mjs` run — the 484-cell baseline split across two machines in seven disjoint parts, and the structural asymmetry it exposed (2026-10-28, the owner's own go and his own machine; the split was his instinct, verbatim: "maybe we should do it in parts so its not one long request", then "ok ill start part2 now", "part4 is here").** This discharges the item owed since 2026-10-19. *(1) The split, and why it is exact.* The harness already carried the mechanism: `--cells a+b,c+d --matrix-only` skips naturals and determinism, and its own header guarantees "the parts' battle sets are disjoint, so their law counters sum exactly". The 484 ordered cells were cut 81+81+81+81+81+79 from the roster list the harness itself prints, verified disjoint and complete, longest command 1,168 characters against cmd.exe's 8,191 limit. Commands live in `scratch/measure-parts.md`; the read-only aggregator is `scratch/measure-agg.mjs` (sums counters, maxes extremes, re-derives winner splits, and asserts the parts share no cell). Parts 1-4 ran on the owner's Windows machine at 8 workers, Parts 5-6 in the sandbox at 2.
*(2) Measured cost, because the header's own estimate was stale.* "~2 hours sequential" predates base damage 1, which made battles roughly ten times longer in ticks. Actual walls: Part 1 73.4 min, Part 2 35.2, Part 3 35.5, Part 4 26.3 (owner, 8 workers, 2.4-6.8 s/battle); Part 6 124.3 min (sandbox, 2 workers, 11.8 s/battle). Per-cell cost varies about threefold by family — projectile-spam cells and mirror cells dominate, and shield cells cannot terminate at all so they burn the full 420s budget each. Sandbox note: two workers measured no faster in aggregate than one (1,778 vs 1,860 fight-ticks/s), so `--jobs` buys nothing on a 2-core box. *(3) The structural finding — damage never scales with spin, in either path.* Blades: `const dealt = a.dmg`. Shots: `const dealt = st.dmg`, snapshotted from `f.dmg` at launch ("Every shot carries the thrower's CURRENT dmg at launch"). Spin reaches damage nowhere; it appears only as event metadata and inside `leadFlip()`. Meanwhile `grow()` feeds two separate accumulators, `spinGain = sk.spin + ab.spin` and `dmgGain = sk.dmg + ab.dmg`. Cross that with the roster, verified weapon by weapon including the check that **orb is melee, not a launcher**:
**the 10 projectile weapons are exactly the 10 weapons carrying `{spin: 0.70, dmg: 0}`, and the 12 melee weapons are exactly the 12 carrying `{spin: 0, dmg: 0.7}`** — a perfect 1:1. Consequence: Speedy (+0.30 spin) holding any projectile weapon has `dmgGain = 0 + 0 = 0`, so **its shots do base damage for the entire battle, forever**, while Heavy (+0.9 dmg) holding the same weapon grows +0.9 per hit. Melee splits +0.7 versus +1.6 per hit. *(4) The fair test of the two skills — like-for-like, shield cells excluded, 384 cells / 6,355 rounds.* Speedy melee vs Heavy melee: **34.4% Speedy** (108 cells, 2,073 rounds). Speedy projectile vs Heavy projectile: **24.9%** (86 cells, 1,125 rounds). Those two diagonals are the only rows that compare the skills with the weapon class held constant, and both favour Heavy — **65.6% of melee mirrors and 75.1% of projectile mirrors**. So the answer to the question the cast entry owed to this run is measured: **+0.30 spin is not equal in value to +0.9 damage, and the two are not even parallel axes** — damage reaches both offence paths, spin reaches neither.
Off-diagonal for scale: Speedy melee vs Heavy projectile 82.7% Speedy; Speedy projectile vs Heavy melee **2.4%**. Weapon class outweighs fighter class by roughly 30 points. *(5) Per-weapon, Speedy's win rate holding the weapon:* scythe 72.2, hammer 69.0, axe 68.4, mace 60.5, nunchaku 60.3, katana 56.0, flail 55.1, sword 54.2, boomerang 48.7, dagger 43.1, orb 39.0, staff 17.8, javelin 14.3, scroll 10.6, bow 9.4, crossbow 6.8, knives 5.6, wand 4.7, tome 4.4 (shield, spear and shuriken arrive with Part 5). Group gap: **melee 55.7% versus projectile 12.0%, 43.3 points**. Boomerang is the one projectile exception. The cause is in the shot ledger — Part 1 alone threw 415,580 shots, landed 38,300 and lost 243,208 to the rim, so **about 9% of shots ever reach a body**. *(6) The shield cannot win a round, and its trait is inert.* Its own player-facing `line` reads `'parries only · no projectile'` and the sim gates it to knock-only contact ("projectile spines, the shield … deals NO spine damage, whatever its hold"), so this is deliberate design rather than a defect. Measured consequence: **Heavy holding the shield won 0 of 229 rounds.** And because `grow()` fires only on a damaging hit, the shield's `trait: { spin: 0.0, dmg: 0.7 }` **can never activate** — it is inert data, as is its wielder's fighter skill.
This is also the mechanism behind open question 1's `shield+shield` non-termination: neither side is capable of a KO, so the cell can only ever exhaust the budget. Open question 4 ("what is a weapon that cannot damage *for*?") now carries that number. *(7) The two live reds at matrix scale.* **Law 3's band class** — overlaps NOT explained by the mirror mechanism — measured 14.673 / 20.754 / 14.488 / 17.604 / 15.745px worst across the five landed slices **[COMPLETED 2026-10-28: Part 5 adds 13.956px, so all six slices are red — worst 20.754px, 3.5× the disclosed limit]**, against a disclosed band of <6px and a 22.83px floor measured with the resolver disabled. It appears in every slice including Part 6, which contains no chain weapon at all, so the "the mirror test is too narrow for chain flips" reading does not cover it; frequency stays trivial (2,877 visible overlaps in 72.8M fight-ticks, 0.006-0.01% per slice), so this is a tail-depth question, not a rate question. It re-opens the katana+shuriken rework item's dilemma at 2.4-3.5x the numbers: either the band's 6px line moves with a recorded justification, or the resolver does. **Projectile law 4s** printed `double departures 3`, confined to Part 1 — zero across the 811,998 shots of Parts 2, 3, 4 and 6. **[SUPERSEDED 2026-10-28 when Part 5 landed: they are NOT confined to Part 1. Part 5 carries 2 more, for 5 across the complete baseline — 1 in ~296,475 of 1,482,374 shots thrown — with Parts 2, 3, 4 and 6 still at zero. Part 6 threw 310,411 shots and found none, so shot volume does not predict them.]**
The label is wrong: the counter is `shotDeathDup` and it conflates `addGone()` firing twice for one sid (line 372) with a shot marked gone yet still in flight at battle end (line 769); neither is a departure. Shot ids are provably battle-unique (`++G.shotSid`, and the sim's own comment "sids never repeat"), so id reuse is ruled out; the leading hypothesis is same-tick ordering — a rim/land/swat death landing in the same tick as a round roll's `clearedSids` sweep, or as the battle end. Damage-side laws are spotless, so no gameplay effect is indicated. *(8) Both reds are un-attributed, and that is the owed next step.* The harness reports counts and worst values but never says where: no cell, no seed, no tick. Localising either needs an attribution edit — cell/seed/tick for the band-class worst overlap, and cell/seed/tick plus which of the two mechanisms for each `shotDeathDup` — applied after the baseline so all seven parts come from one instrument version, then a re-run of only the implicated slices. *(9) Integrity, and the caveat.* Across **3,224 battles · 6,935 rounds · 193,523 hits · 151,527 parries · 72,801,424 rendered fight-ticks · 1,363,242,755 substep poses** (403 of 484 cells, 83.3%):
phantom hits 0/193,523, phantom shot hits 0/97,073, swats without touch 0/74,006, parries without touch 0/151,527, substep-budget violations 0, off-point launches 0, melee hits by projectile weapons 0, **missing chain poses 0** — so every law recompute used the sim's own stored poses and none silently degraded to the rigid spine. All of it is measured at base damage 1 against 100 hp, which the owner called "just a test": absolute win rates will move when those constants move, but the structural facts will not — spin feeds no damage path, the projectile half of the roster inherits only its fighter's dmg, and the shield's trait can never fire. Part files and md5s: `scratch/m-01.txt` `4e0e06f4b938ab5d9a97f4688a55635f`, `m-02.txt` `b4334de39118bf4227bb6c31af41f7e0`, `m-03.txt` `80d2dc1ea508f305cb53a0db1e77110e`, `m-04.txt` `129bd14e419b51b06f39667eb4df8323`, `m-06.txt` `fc35bf31ae3e65ec68026d867fd2af51`, plus the owner's calibration capture `scratch/measure-quick-20261028-owner.txt` `0295f2030ee2ed896cf1d24724253b4b` **[file RETIRED in the 2026-10-28 pre-skills cleanup, owner go — the md5 above and its conclusion, that his Windows machine reproduced the sandbox number-for-number, are the surviving record; the artifact is not]**; Part 5 (`m-05.txt`) was in flight at the time of writing. **[ALL SIX part files RETIRED from the workspace later on 2026-10-28, owner go — he holds local copies. The md5s above survive as the identity record and will let him verify those copies; `scratch/baseline-484.csv` + `baseline-laws.csv` are the queryable form, cross-checked to reproduce all 14 published totals. Nothing in the workspace reads the raw files.]**

## Retired Measured-facts entries — the pre-`review-r32` closed-design ledger (2026-10-28, whole-file code-review cleanup #5 at owner order "trim old text or sections we no longer need"; verbatim, append-only)

Three entries retired from `uploads/GAMES.md`'s Measured facts, each leaving an indexed tombstone there that names this section and its live successor. Retired because the subject had closed and a live document carries the residue — an estimate replaced by the built thing, a ruling that changed no code, and an intermediate value in a three-build same-day sequence. Nothing here is a live claim; every number that still binds is named in the tombstone's successor pointer, so the trim cannot silently orphan one.

Index (old GAMES.md line → entry):

- L444 — Skills-prep cost map — what a per-weapon skill actually has to touch, measured before any…
- L505 — The dagger's growth axis ruled — +0.7 DMG stands, and the ruling changed no code…
- L564 — The spear's growth rate raised to +35% — `lengrow-r27`, on the owner's "more ridiculous"…
- *(reference-section line, not an entry)* GAMES.md **"Standing baseline:"** line in *Fingerprints — how to re-run* — an 8,927-byte chain of per-build `[Extended …]`/`[Unchanged …]`/`[Still unchanged …]` annotations accreted from `lead-r22` through `pair-r31`, retired as one block below.

### Retired from GAMES.md L444 (2026-10-28)

*Why it was retired: a pre-build cost estimate, superseded by the canonical's own `TRAIT / SKILL REGISTRY` block and by three measured landings (`skill-r29`, `seam-r30`, `pair-r31`); its spin-headroom paragraph survives in the dedicated `omegaCap` entry.*

**Skills-prep cost map — what a per-weapon skill actually has to touch, measured before any of it is built (2026-10-28, pre-skills cleanup; analysis only, no code changed, no plumbing added).** Written so the skills pass budgets correctly instead of discovering the expensive axis mid-design. **The growth site is already per-weapon.** `grow()` is the single place growth happens: it reads `FIGHTERS[idx].skill` and `WEAPONS[weapon].ability` ({spin, dmg}) and applies both on every damaging hit. Per-weapon skills are therefore a KEYING change, not a rewrite. **What a NEW axis costs** (the checklist, derived from the two that exist): a fighter field plus its per-round reset; the consumer site that reads it at hit or launch time; the legend/poster SPD-DMG boxes, which are a fixed three-channel display, so a fourth channel is a layout change; the `G.maxDmg` / `maxSpin` ridiculousness trackers; the fingerprint's hashed fields; and harness coverage — a new axis needs its own probe the way `lead` needed leadproof, or it ships unproven (the facing pass's vacuous-unextended-run trap is the precedent: a harness that passes without covering the new flag proves nothing).
**Cheap axes — launch-snapshotted.** Shot `r`, `speed`, `turn`, `bounce` and `len` are each copied onto the projectile at birth exactly like `dmg: f.dmg`, so a per-fighter multiplier is one line per axis with no boot-time consequence and no geometry rebuild. Knock is one hit-time read of a per-fighter field (today a global `CFG.hitKnock` 130). *[Corrected 2026-10-28: this entry originally named the field `CFG.knock` — the identifier was wrong, and the two skills-model entries that quoted it were wrong the same way; the value (130) and the analysis are unchanged. Measured while grounding the skill list: `CFG.hitKnock` is read at exactly two sites, `bladeVsBody` and `shotHitBody`, so knock is live on all 22 weapons.]* Any of these can be a skill without touching the bake. **Expensive axis — length growth, which the owner ruled 2026-10-28 IS in the skill vision.** `W.len` is consumed at BOOT to build SPAN, REACH, the scaled polys and the baked covering circles, so runtime growth needs a per-fighter length scale threaded through `spineFrame`, `bladeCircles` and the renderer, and it invalidates two boot-derived bounds: REACH (the parryGap prune bound) and MINR (the adaptive-substep budget), both computed once from authored len. The boot LEN_MIN/LT gate would NOT catch runtime growth, so the ceiling has to live inside the growth rule itself. Scoped, deliberately not built: the rule's shape (per hit? per round? capped where?) decides the plumbing, so nothing is threaded until the skill list exists.
This is the axis the owner's spectacle example named — "lets imagine spear geting longer each hit, suddenly it is hilariously long" — and it is the only one needing engineering rather than data. **Spin headroom (the ceiling the owner asked about).** `omegaCap: 140` is a sim-stability clamp, not a gameplay cap. Observed maximum across 30 naturals is 103.5 rad/s = base 3.5 + exactly 100 damaging hits × 1.0/hit (ability 0.70 + Speedy 0.30), so the clamp bites at 136.5 hits on that axis: **36.5 hits of headroom, and it clamps silently** — no readout, no event, the fighter just stops accelerating. Four options were put to the owner (leave it silent and record the headroom / measure the true stability limit read-only / raise it, which is a sim change owing its own kept pair / promote it to a visible gameplay cap); **the owner picked the measurement, and it superseded the framing above: the clamp is dormant, the binding ceiling is hp ÷ damage, and the stability limit is ω ≈ 258 at the worst pair — see the spin-ceiling measurement entry. The constant is no longer folklore.** **Launcher readiness.** After this landing the per-weapon shot surface is `revs` / `volley` / `cycle` / `copy` plus r/speed/turn/bounce/len/kind, all on the row: a skill that changes shot behaviour edits data or snapshots a per-fighter multiplier at launch, never the launcher.
**Owed before skills — SUPERSEDED 2026-10-28 by the no-balancing ruling: this now runs at the END of the skills pass, not before it.** The sanctioned `measure.mjs` run on the owner's machine (its 19 forced cells miss the dagger and the staff, and its expected counts predate both damage 1 and the facing pass) and the kit-route rebuild, which must carry the placement/reach rules and WDEF.

### Retired from GAMES.md L505 (2026-10-28)

*Why it was retired: a ruling that closed with no code change (+0.7 DMG was already the shipped value), so no mechanism is left to describe; its discharge notes live in Open design questions 6 and the roster row.*

**The dagger's growth axis ruled — +0.7 DMG stands, and the ruling changed no code (2026-10-28, owner go verbatim: "ok go", on the recommendation "+0.7 DMG". Of the two owner decisions the completed baseline was waiting on, this is the one that needed no compute.)** *(1) What the question actually was.* Not a magnitude — a **binary axis choice**, carried by the roster table's ⚠ since 2026-10-19. The dagger's trait was +0.7 **SPIN** in the owner-locked v0.7 growth-identity decision; the undocumented era flipped it to +0.7 DMG with no recorded reason, and the owner's 2026-10-28 pre-skills ruling was explicitly *neither ratify nor revert* — the skills pass decides. Under M2 a trait is weapon data, so either value is one number and no plumbing. The two branches: **+0.7 DMG** makes the dagger a Heavy-synergy weapon (Heavy's +0.9 → **1.6 per damaging hit** together) and leaves the roster split at **12 +DMG / 10 +SPIN**; **+0.7 SPIN** restores its v0.7 identity, makes it a Speedy-synergy weapon (+0.30 → **1.00/hit**, the cell that produced the measured ω 103.5 maximum) and moves the split to **11/11**.
*(2) The ruling, and the datum read honestly.* **+0.7 DMG stands.** The datum the deferral was waiting on: the dagger ranks **13th of 22** at a **53.8%** holder win rate — dead average, and the last weapon above the cliff (orb 53.9 · dagger 53.8 · then javelin 27.6). Dead average *under the DMG axis* says the axis is not what mis-tunes the dagger; its rank is better explained by reach (`len: 78`, the shortest melee blade in the roster) than by its growth family. And the revert would have paired it with the fighter that loses **61%** of rounds and with the family whose group holder win% is **29.3** against melee's **69.0** — so the data argues *against* +0.7 SPIN rather than merely failing to argue for +0.7 DMG. *(3) NO SIM CHANGE — and the correction that matters.* The dagger row already ships `trait: { spin: 0.0, dmg: 0.7 }`, byte-identical to the other eleven +DMG weapons (all twelve carry that exact pair). Ratifying the shipped value therefore moves nothing: **canonical md5 `e401e3e33c3133b03ec192f7e5fe0199` unchanged, 3,093 lines, no fingerprint movement, no re-baseline, no stamp bump.** This entry also corrects the framing the ruling was granted under, which described +0.7 DMG as a data-table edit that would move the fingerprints and need them re-baselined.
It would not, because it is the current value; only the **+0.7 SPIN** branch was a behaviour change. The deferral closes in the docs alone: the roster-table ⚠ is marked CLOSED with the ruling and the no-change fact, and the discharged "Next"/"Owed"/"synergy question" claims in the four entries that carried it are bracketed in place with the date, per the house pattern for a paid obligation. *(4) Reversibility, recorded so the alternative stays recoverable.* One number in one row, no plumbing. Had the owner picked SPIN — or picks it later — `dagger.trait` becomes `{ spin: 0.70, dmg: 0.0 }`, and that IS a sanctioned sim change: fingerprints re-baselined to a new `.qa/fp-<stamp>.json`, stamp bumped, roster split 11/11. *(5) What is still owed.* The other decision does need the owner's machine: the attribution re-runs (Part 2 then Part 1, commands in `scratch/measure-parts.md`), and after them the **band-vs-resolver** ruling for law 3. Then the skills pass itself, starting with the length-growth rule's shape — design, not code, since under M2 the hook machinery is not built until a special skill exists to hang on it.

### Retired from GAMES.md L564 (2026-10-28)

*Why it was retired: a same-day rate raise superseded within hours by `lengrow-r28` (+200%), which carries the shipped ladder, the per-step re-baked coverings and the remap ceiling; the rate history stays in the doctrine section "Length growth — the spear's trait".*

**The spear's growth rate raised to +35% — `lengrow-r27`, on the owner's "more ridiculous", bounded by a ceiling that was swept before the number was picked (2026-10-28; owner: *"i want it to be more ridiculous, so increase the growth of the spear even more"*, the magnitude chosen from a measured menu via ask_user and the step count delegated to me. Canonical md5 `fc8ca35c3204261b703555f585f70c75` → **`f50612a2e954e7fd45b819234bada1ba`**, build `lengrow-r26` → **`lengrow-r27`**, lines 3,205 → **3,217**. Nine lines out, twenty-one in: **exactly three are executable** — the spear row and the two stamp sites — and the other eighteen are comment.)**
*(1) The ceiling was measured first, and it is a hard number.* With the covering held — which is what the shipped ladder does, every `l` and every `r` bit-identical at every step — the binding band is **MISS**, not POKE. A sweep of shaft factors through `.qa/lengrow-gate.mjs` puts the last passing step at **k 1.6469 = tip +39.5%** (miss 4.59 of the 4.60 band) and the first failure at **+40%** (4.61). POKE never binds anywhere on the sweep (-0.26…-0.30px against a +0.12px band), `minR` stays 2.20 and n stays 19 throughout, because the head cluster translates rigidly and the radii are held. So "how ridiculous can the spear get" has a measured answer of **about +40% total tip growth**, set by the baked circle spacing along the shaft — exactly the mechanism the covering-ceiling table in the design section predicted, at a different number, because that table measured a *uniform* stretch.
*(2) The two routes past +40%, and why neither was needed.* Beyond it the covering must follow the growth: either **densify per step at boot** (interpolate extra circles wherever the remapped spacing outruns the held radii — no authored data, but the spear's circle count grows and with it the collision broadphase cost) or **re-bake the twelve rows** through `inscribe.mjs --regen` + `bake-rows.mjs` (the route the original design recorded; sanctioned and offline, at the cost of ~12 × 19 circles of data in the canonical). The owner picked **+35%**, which needs neither — one number in one row. Both routes stay open if he wants more later, and the swept ceiling is now written into the canonical's own LENGTH GROWTH comment block so the next session does not have to re-derive it.
*(3) The change, and the step count I kept.* `trait.len` **0.012 → 0.0253**, legend **"grows 1.2% longer per damaging hit" → "grows 2.5% longer per damaging hit"** (same character count, so the legend layout does not move). 0.0253 was taken over the rounder 0.025 because both measure **identically at the binding step** (miss 4.50 at the top) while 0.0253 lands **+34.96%** against 0.025's +34.49% — closer to the picked target for free. **`LEN_STEPS` stays 12**, the delegated call: the ceiling bounds *total* growth, so more steps at this rate would sail past +39.5%, and fewer would plateau early and stop reading as a snowball. With ~14 damaging hits per fighter per round a 12-step ladder tops out near the round's end, so the growth runs the whole round — which is the spectacle the owner is buying.
*(4) The rebuilt ladder, measured — `.qa/lengrow-build-proof.mjs`, **42/42**, exit 0.* Drawn tip 147.472 → **199.031px (+34.96%)** · shaft factors 1.0414 → **1.5726**, derived per step from the tip factor and never authored · still never thickens: **456 `l`/`r` values bit-identical**, lateral envelope unchanged to 1e-12 · no gap opens (shaft end **149.61** ≥ head rear **136.88** at the top step) · `REACH.spear` 210.412 → **261.971** (+51.56px, **+24.5%**) and `MINR.spear` still **2.20** · the shared `WEAPONS.spear` row still unmutated · the outermost covering circle at the top step reaches x **754.799** (725.935 at +15.4%). Two assertions were added and one **de-hardcoded**: the top-step target now derives from the row's own `trait.len` instead of a literal `1.012^12` — that literal went stale in this very edit — and a new assertion pins the top step under the swept +39.5% ceiling, so a future rate raise fails the proof in eleven seconds instead of failing the gate later.
*(5) The house gate on the exact factors the build now carries.* `inscribe.mjs` exits **0**, all 22 weapons inside band, spear base row unchanged at poke -0.30px / miss 4.25px. `lengrow-gate --mode rigid`, fed the thirteen shaft factors **read out of the live build**, passes every step: n=19 and minR 2.20 constant, poke -0.26…-0.30px, miss **4.18…4.50px** against the 4.6px band — worst margin **0.10px**, at the top step only, every other step at 4.30 or better. leadproof (32s), flipproof (24s) and chromecheck (10s) all PASS, exit 0. Both script blocks re-parse under `new Function`. (chromecheck's `legend=816B` is **not** a signal — its byte counts are unreproducible by the defect recorded in the `stall-r25` entry, and 816 sits inside the 806-823 wander measured on an unchanged file.)
*(6) Fingerprints: 0 of 8 moved again, for the same vacuous reason.* md5 `d120f6c525a1bd5f3d35459af699a171` on both sides, so `.qa/fp-stall-r25.json` still stands and again no twin file was written — and again that result is **blind**, not reassuring: the suite still never rolls a spear, so no fingerprint can characterise a length-growth edit at any rate. The evidence is the growth-off differential in section N, which diverges at **t=286 / t=290 / t=286** on the three forced-spear cells with the control pinned at step 0.
*(7) One balance signal REVERSED, and it is good news.* At +15.4% the `spear+sword s21` differential ran the **full 18,000-tick budget undecided** while its growth-off control decided 0-2 — the longer spear had made that battle *less* decisive, and I flagged it as a stall risk. At +35% the same cell **decides**, at 13,226 ticks, and the other two shortened as well (14,689 → **10,351** and 16,222 → **8,834** rows). More reach means more contacts actually land, which is the opposite of the stall axis. Three cells is not a trend and the 484-cell re-run is still the authority — but the direction is worth recording before it, because it suggests length growth is a stall *reliever* rather than a stall *cause*, and the owner's ruling that round-ending stalls stay deliberate until skills is unaffected either way.
*(8) Docs and tool hygiene.* In the design section: the rate paragraph and the trilemma paragraph carry dated amendments, the heading and the status line name `lengrow-r27`, and the superseded uniform-stretch numbers (+18.6%, ~228px reach, "1.2% × 14") are **left verbatim as history** with the amendment stating the measured replacement. Current state and the "Next" bullet are updated, and the standing-baseline paragraph records the second no-move. `.qa/lengrow-gate.mjs` and `.qa/lengrow-proof.mjs` had their **default `rate` updated to 0.0253** so the tools track the shipped row instead of silently defaulting to the old one. `scratch/docreflow.mjs` FIRST, then `scratch/navgen.mjs` (45 → 46 entries).
*(9) Owed now.* Unchanged in kind, sharper in degree: the **484-cell re-run** covers `stall-r25` + `lengrow-r27` in one pass, and law 3's band should be expected to widen **more** than the `lengrow-r26` entry predicted, because the spear's reach is now +24.5% over base rather than +10.8% · **a spear-bearing fingerprint cell**, owed twice over · the shot-cross gap · the **rotational-DOF resolver rework**, now to be designed against a **261.97px** spear reach · the first special skill · elemental design · the kit-route rebuild. And if the owner wants past +40%, the densify-or-rebake decision in (2) becomes the next build.

### Retired from GAMES.md — the "Standing baseline:" annotation chain (2026-10-28)

*Why it was retired: this single line had accreted one bracketed annotation per build for nine consecutive landings (`lead-r22` → `pair-r31`), reaching 8,927 bytes in one paragraph — the largest single line in the file. Every one of those annotations was a **dated record of a fingerprint result that has since been superseded or repeated**: the `fp-lead-r22` claims were reversed by the `stall-r25` re-baselining, the four "vacuous 8/8" warnings for `lengrow-r26/27/28` and `skill-r29` are restated compactly in Measured-facts entry #51 item (7), and the `seam-r30`/`pair-r31` "meaningful vs silent-by-construction" distinctions are preserved in their own entries and in the pair-proof record. The live question a reader asks of this section — *which baseline file describes the shipped build, and when may a new one be written?* — needed four lines, not nine thousand characters. Retired verbatim below so no dated fact is lost; GAMES.md now carries the compact live answer plus a pointer here.*

> **Verbatim, as it stood in `uploads/GAMES.md` before the `review-r32` doc pass:**
>
> **Standing baseline:** the standing fingerprint baseline is **`.qa/fp-lead-r22.json`** (the facing pass — dagger and staff flagged `lead: -1`, build `lead-r22`, landed 2026-10-28) — and the pre-skills cleanup that followed it (the launcher consolidation; canonical `54ab0e5b784962987fdec6cc71cd6636`) moved NO fingerprint, 8/8 byte-identical with md5 `f1de581b7ec18be83af57d94ca592e5a` on both sides, so this baseline still describes the shipped build and no seventh file was written for it — and the sigil-legibility landing that followed (`sigil-r23`, canonical `c4483833621f5f01353cb821d5ca11b2`) is renderer-only and likewise moved no fingerprint, so `.qa/fp-lead-r22.json` still describes the shipped build **even though the build stamp has moved on**. **The four historical checkpoints behind it — `fp-dmg1-r21.json` (its before-half, base damage 1), `fp-authoring.json` (byte-identical to that, md5 `13a9b296a9f41bee4b31fd7bcd910212` for both), `fp-shotvocab.json` and `fp-pause-after.json` (the `pause-r20` dmg-5 baseline) — were DELETED 2026-10-28 in the pre-skills workspace cleanup (owner go), so `fp-lead-r22.json` is now the only baseline file on disk. This reverses the earlier decision recorded here to keep `fp-authoring.json` because a dated entry cites it by name: the citations, the md5s and the four proven pairs all stay in the record, the artifacts do not. The stray default-path `.qa/fingerprint.json` was likewise deleted — `fingerprint.mjs` regenerates it on demand and it is scratch output, never a baseline.** Refactor-neutrality runs diff before/after against the standing baseline, and only a sanctioned sim-data landing re-baselines it (keeping its before/after pair as the proof); the vocabulary rename was the one exception — behaviour-identical, but event keys are hash inputs, so every cell moved and neutrality was proven from the numeric harnesses instead (cellscan/natscan/projectile-fix identical). `.qa/baseline-sweep-r15.html` was the byte-exact pre-clinch canonical copy kept for the balance-rework's law-drift attribution — *purged 2026-10-28 (hygiene pass #2, owner pick): that attribution job CLOSED the same day, and every finding the baseline existed to produce is recorded in the `chainlaw` entry below; its digest `abd707d708ef607c9534f82e23fa5074` is on file in the archive's retired section.* **The read-only diagnosis aids `seedtrace.mjs`, `rollattr.mjs` and `cellscan.mjs` were retired in the same 2026-10-28 cleanup.** **[Extended 2026-10-28, build `cross-r24`:** the shot-cross `break` also moved NO fingerprint, 8/8 byte-identical, so `.qa/fp-lead-r22.json` still describes the shipped build and no twin file was written. **But this one is NOT evidence of behaviour-neutrality** — the suite runs the natural tier at seeds 1-11 while the double-kills it fixes were found at forced-matrix seeds 101/102/106, at 1 in ~138,527 shots, so the 8 cells never exercise the shot-cross path at all. **Coverage gap, fix owed:** this baseline is blind to shot-vs-shot changes, and any future "behaviour-identical" claim resting on it cannot cover them.]** **[Re-baselined 2026-10-28, build `stall-r25`:** the resolver's inverted stall test at :1708 is the **first landing since `lead-r22` to MOVE fingerprints** — 7 of 8 cells changed, only `natural s7` byte-identical — so `.qa/fp-lead-r22.json` **no longer describes the shipped build** and is superseded by **`.qa/fp-stall-r25.json`** (canonical `cd5f4de10e15c35fc4e97ac579f47673`). This is precisely the case the rule above provides for — *"only a sanctioned sim-data landing re-baselines it (keeping its before/after pair as the proof)"* — and the pair is kept: `fp-lead-r22.json` is the before-half, `fp-stall-r25.json` the after. That **reverses** the "only baseline file on disk" statement above, by adding a second file rather than by deleting one. The **shot-cross coverage gap is inherited unchanged**, since the new baseline runs the same 8 cells; it is still owed. Undecided cells in the suite went 3 → 4 (`hammer+dagger s5` 13777 → the 18000-tick budget) while two cells decided *faster*, so the 8-cell sample cannot characterise the effect on the 4.9%-undecided headline — the 484-cell re-run is owed and should cover `stall-r25` together with the length-growth build.]** **[Unchanged 2026-10-28, build `lengrow-r26`:** the length-growth build moved **no** fingerprint — 8/8 byte-identical, both files md5 `d120f6c525a1bd5f3d35459af699a171` — so `.qa/fp-stall-r25.json` still describes the shipped build and **no twin file was written**, per the rule above that a landing which moves nothing earns no new artifact (the `sigil-r23`/`cross-r24` precedent). A generated `.qa/fp-lengrow-r26.json` was confirmed byte-identical and **deleted rather than adopted**: two role-names for one artifact would have cost a slot in README's 10-file Batch 1 for zero information. **But this 8/8 is VACUOUS and is not evidence of behaviour-neutrality** — six cells are forced non-spear matchups and neither natural cell rolls a spear (measured: s7 → orb+orb then bow+boomerang, s11 → shield+javelin), so no cell can observe length growth, and `f.lenStep` is not in the hashed field list either. The suite is now blind on two axes, shot-cross and spear. The build's real evidence is `.qa/lengrow-build-proof.mjs` section N, a growth-off control that diverges from the built sim at t≈286 on all three forced-spear cells. **Adding a spear-bearing cell is owed.**]** **[Still unchanged 2026-10-28, build `lengrow-r27`:** the growth-rate raise moved **no** fingerprint either — 0 of 8, md5 `d120f6c525a1bd5f3d35459af699a171` on both sides — so `.qa/fp-stall-r25.json` still stands and still no twin file was written. The evidence for `r27` is again `.qa/lengrow-build-proof.mjs` (42/42), whose section N differential diverges at t=286/290/286 on three forced-spear cells, plus the house gate and `lengrow-gate --mode rigid` on the exact built factors. **A spear-bearing cell is now owed twice over.**]** **[Still unchanged 2026-10-28, builds `lengrow-r27` and `lengrow-r28`:** neither moved a fingerprint — 0 of 8 each, `.qa/fp-stall-r25.json` md5 `d120f6c525a1bd5f3d35459af699a171` unchanged — so it still stands and still no twin file was written. **This is now the third consecutive build where 8/8 means nothing**: the suite cannot see a spear at any length, and `lengrow-r28` triples one. No twin file written. `skill-r29` is also the build that proves fingerprints cannot cover a ladder raise even in principle: `REACH` is a per-weapon table, so raising the ladder top changes the substep budget of every spear battle, carrier or not.]** **[And `seam-r30` is the FIFTH consecutive 0-of-8 — but this one is MEANINGFUL rather than vacuous, which is worth marking because the previous four were not. The shot-cadence seam ships inert (no row declares a multiplier) and `brief` is exported metadata rather than sim state, so the landing is behaviour-identical BY CONSTRUCTION; and unlike the length-growth builds, the suite does cover launchers (bow+crossbow, wand+bow, boomerang+shuriken), so 8/8 is real evidence of no-change here. It remains blind to the spear, and `seam-r30` changed nothing a spear would show.]** **[And `pair-r31` is the SIXTH consecutive 0-of-8 — meaningful for one half of the change and *silent by construction* on the other, which is a new shape of gap worth naming. The fingerprint suite plays `DEFAULT_CAST = [0, 1]`, where `f.slot` and `f.idx` hold the same number, so 8/8 is genuine evidence that the shipped pairing did not move (the cells do cover shots, kills and the slot-keyed wins path). But **no fingerprint cell can ever exercise a swapped pairing**, so 8/8 says nothing at all about whether `pair-r31`'s 28 slot-sites are right — that is `.qa/pair-proof.mjs`'s whole job (31/31, 103,076 ticks swept, and the damage-per-hit rates 0.70/1.60 measured to exchange sides under `cast: [1, 0]`). Rule this leaves behind: **a mechanism that ships inert under its default needs a probe that runs it non-default**, or the standing suite will keep reporting a pass it is not entitled to.]**
